1) TL;DR
The guide is technically strong and broadly accurate; most formulas and algorithms are correct at the conceptual level. The main issues are a handful of off‑by‑one/edge cases in viewport/scroll math, a few inconsistent field semantics (notably `preferredColumn` and `Selection`), some pseudocode gaps (missing params, globals), and a few important but fixable omissions (folding + wrapping interaction, IME/composition, bidi). Overall changes are **S–M (1–3h)** if you focus on the concrete fixes below.

---

## 1. Mathematical formulas

### Scrollbars and basic transforms

- **Scrollable extent** (lines 50–53)
  ```js
  scrollableHeight = contentHeight - viewportHeight
  scrollableWidth  = contentWidth - viewportWidth
  ```
  Conceptually correct, but:
  - If `contentHeight <= viewportHeight`, `scrollableHeight` is negative.
    You later handle this with clamping (lines 2063–2066); it would help to note here that callers should clamp to `>= 0` or use `max(0, …)` directly in the definition:
    ```js
    scrollableHeight = Math.max(0, contentHeight - viewportHeight)
    ```

- **Thumb size** (lines 68–72)
  ```js
  thumbLength / trackLength = viewportSize / contentSize
  ```
  Mathematically correct. Later you discuss minimum thumb size (lines 2147–2151). I’d add one explicit note here:
  - If `contentSize <= viewportSize`, treat it as “no scroll” (thumb at full track or hidden) and avoid division by zero.

- **Scroll ↔ thumb position** (lines 76–80)
  ```js
  scrollOffset / scrollableExtent = thumbPosition / (trackLength - thumbLength)
  ```
  Conceptually fine for proportional scrollbars, but you’re missing two critical edge cases:
  - `scrollableExtent == 0` (no scrollable content) → division by zero.
  - `trackLength == thumbLength` (thumb fills track) → denominator `trackLength - thumbLength` is zero.
  Recommendation:
  - Call out that for `scrollableExtent <= 0` you should fix `scrollOffset = 0`, `thumbPosition = 0`.
  - Either define this only for `contentSize > viewportSize` or include explicit guards.

- **Content ↔ viewport transform** (lines 84–87)
  ```js
  viewportY = contentY - scrollOffsetY
  contentY  = viewportY + scrollOffsetY
  ```
  Correct.

### Viewport line calculations

- **First/last visible line** (non‑wrapped) (lines 422–428)
  ```js
  firstVisibleLine = floor(scrollOffsetY / lineHeight)
  lastVisibleLine = floor((scrollOffsetY + viewportHeight) / lineHeight)
  ```
  This is **off by one** if you interpret `lastVisibleLine` as an inclusive index of visible lines.

  Example (0‑indexed, `lineHeight = 10`, `viewportHeight = 30`, `scrollOffsetY = 0`):
  - Visible lines are 0, 1, 2 (spanning [0,30)).
  - Above formula gives:
    - `firstVisibleLine = floor(0/10) = 0`
    - `lastVisibleLine = floor(30/10) = 3` → points to the **first line completely below** the viewport.

  Two options:
  - Make `lastVisibleLine` inclusive and fix formula:
    ```js
    lastVisibleLine = floor((scrollOffsetY + viewportHeight - 1) / lineHeight)
    // or equivalently:
    lastVisibleLine = ceil((scrollOffsetY + viewportHeight) / lineHeight) - 1
    ```
  - Or define `lastVisibleLineExclusive` as:
    ```js
    lastVisibleLineExclusive = ceil((scrollOffsetY + viewportHeight) / lineHeight)
    ```
    and be explicit about inclusive/exclusive semantics.

- **Fully vs partially visible lines** (lines 433–438)
  ```js
  firstFullyVisibleLine = ceil(scrollOffsetY / lineHeight)
  lastFullyVisibleLine = floor((scrollOffsetY + viewportHeight) / lineHeight) - 1
  // Or:
  lastFullyVisibleLine = floor((scrollOffsetY + viewportHeight - lineHeight) / lineHeight)
  ```
  These are consistent if `lineHeight` is constant and indices are 0‑based. Good.

- **getVisibleDocumentLines() without wrapping** (lines 849–853)
  ```js
  return {
      first: floor(scrollOffsetY / lineHeight),
      last: ceil((scrollOffsetY + viewportHeight) / lineHeight)
  }
  ```
  Same issue as above:
  - As written, `last` is **one past the last visible index** (i.e., an exclusive end). That’s fine if you document it as such and use it consistently; currently the term “last” suggests inclusive.
  Suggested clarification:
  ```js
  firstInclusive = floor(scrollOffsetY / lineHeight)
  lastExclusive  = ceil((scrollOffsetY + viewportHeight) / lineHeight)
  ```

### Binary search helpers & visual lines

- **binarySearchFloor / Ceil** (lines 2566–2604)
  - `binarySearchFloor` returns 0 for targets below the first value (clamped) – that’s intentional and good for hit‑testing (clicking above content maps to first line).
  - `binarySearchCeil` returns `array.length - 1` for targets above the last value – similarly clamped to the last line. Also fine.

- **getVisibleLines with wrapping** (lines 868–878)
  ```js
  const firstVisual = binarySearchFloor(visualLineOffsets, scrollOffsetY)
  const lastVisual = binarySearchCeil(visualLineOffsets, scrollOffsetY + viewportHeight)
  ```
  Here `visualLineOffsets` are the **tops** of visual lines. With the Ceil helper, `lastVisual` becomes the index of the first line with `top >= bottomOfViewport`. That line is **not actually visible** unless its top is exactly at the bottom.

  - If you intend a `[firstInclusive, lastExclusive)` range, you should:
    ```js
    const firstVisual = binarySearchFloor(visualLineOffsets, scrollOffsetY)
    const lastExclusive = binarySearchCeil(visualLineOffsets, scrollOffsetY + viewportHeight)
    ```
    and name it that way.
  - If you intend an inclusive last index, use:
    ```js
    const lastVisual = binarySearchFloor(visualLineOffsets, scrollOffsetY + viewportHeight - 1)
    ```

  As written, any drawing loop `for (vl = firstVisual; vl <= lastVisual; vl++)` will render **one extra line beyond the viewport**. That may be acceptable as a “buffer” line, but it’s worth calling out or renaming to avoid confusion.

### Line number width

- **computeLineNumberWidth** (lines 371–375)
  ```js
  const digitCount = Math.floor(Math.log10(lineCount)) + 1
  ```
  This explodes for `lineCount <= 0` (log10(0) is `-∞`). Edge case is small but easy to fix:
  ```js
  const safeLineCount = Math.max(1, lineCount)
  const digitCount = Math.floor(Math.log10(safeLineCount)) + 1
  ```

---

## 2. Data structures

Overall, the data structures are **well‑chosen and align with real editors**. A few points:

### Document & offsets

- **Document.lineOffsets** (lines 172–175)
  ```js
  lineOffsets: Array<Number>  // byte offset where each line starts
  ```
  - For UTF‑8 on disk vs UTF‑16 in memory, “byte offset” can be misleading. Many modern editors track:
    - **buffer offsets** (e.g., UTF‑16 code units), and
    - **file offsets** (bytes in encoded representation) separately.
  Suggest: rename or comment that this is “buffer offset (code units)”, unless you really mean raw bytes.

### EditorState & Cursor

- **EditorState.scrollOffset** (lines 193–202) – fine.

- **Cursor.preferredColumn** (lines 209–213)
  ```js
  preferredColumn: Number     // remembered column for vertical movement
  ```
  Later in **6.8 Impact on Cursor Movement** (lines 891–895) you say:
  ```js
  // preferredColumn stores pixel X, not column
  const targetX = cursor.preferredColumn ??
                  computeXOffset(cursor.column, currentSegment)
  ```
  This is an important **semantic inconsistency**:

  - At the type level you describe it as a “column”.
  - In the movement code you treat it as a **pixel X** value.

  Suggested fix:
  - Either:
    - Rename field everywhere to `preferredX` or `preferredVisualX`, and document that it’s in pixels; or
    - Store true **logical column** and recompute pixel X per line (`computeXOffset(preferredColumn, segment)`).

  The pixel‑based approach is more common for proportional fonts; if you keep that, update the earlier definition and docs.

### Selection

- **Selection type** (lines 223–227)
  ```js
  Selection {
      anchor: Position
      head: Position
  }
  ```
  But in selection rendering (lines 1050–1054, 1071–1072) you reference `selection.start` / `selection.end` and `selection.head` in other places:
  ```js
  if (documentLine === selection.start.line) { ... }
  if (documentLine === selection.end.line) { ... }

  if (documentLine < selection.end.line &&
      segmentIndex === getSegments(documentLine).length - 1) { ... }

  // Later (lines 2236–2239):
  ensureCursorVisible(selection.head)
  ```
  This is a **terminology mismatch**: the core type uses `anchor/head`, some code snippets use `start/end`, and others use `head`. Suggest:

  - Standardize on **one canonical shape**:
    - Either `anchor/head` with a derived `normalizedStart/end`, or
    - `start/end` plus a `direction` or `activeEnd` flag.
  - In code examples, use the same names as the struct to avoid confusion.

### Visual line / wrapping structures

- `DocumentLine`, `VisualLineSegment`, `VisualLineMapping`, and `VisualLineIndex` (lines 604–632, 779–797) are well-structured and match how real editors model wrapping and visual lines.

Two completeness notes:

- You allow per‑segment `height` (lines 622–623) but in `buildVisualIndex` (lines 803–838) you always do:
  ```js
  yOffset += lineHeight
  ```
  ignoring `segment.height`. Either:
  - Assume fixed `lineHeight` and drop the `height` field from `VisualLineSegment`, or
  - Use `segment.height` and document how variable line heights (inline widgets, code lenses) are handled.

- **Folding & hidden regions**:
  `EditorState` has `foldedRanges` (line 196), but the visual index and wrapping logic never mention folding. In practice, folded regions must:
  - Be excluded from `visualLineOffsets`, `documentToVisual`, `visualToDocument`.
  - Affect scrollable height and hit‑testing.
  This is an important missing integration point (see §6 below).

### Overlays / popups

- `PopupAnchor` / `AutocompletePopup` / `OverlayManager` (lines 1165–1213, 1540–1595) are structurally sound and mirror real systems (VS Code, Monaco).

- Slight API mismatch in `positionPopup` signature (line 1219) vs its usage:
  ```js
  function positionPopup(popup, viewport, cursor): PopupPlacement
  // but inside it uses:
  const anchorPoint = documentToViewport(popup.anchor.documentPosition)
  // `cursor` parameter is unused.
  ```
  Later overwritten usages treat the third argument as a viewport position (lines 1399–1404, 1462–1469). I’d either:
  - Drop the unused parameter from the signature, or
  - Change to `positionPopup(popup, viewport, anchorViewportPoint)` and use that instead of recomputing.

---

## 3. Algorithms and edge cases

### Cursor–viewport alignment

- **computeMinimalScroll** (lines 555–569)
  This is logically correct for line‑based margins:
  - When cursor above safe zone, new scroll puts cursor at the **top margin**.
  - When below, it puts cursor bottom at the **bottom margin**.

  Missing but important details:
  - **Clamping**: result should be clamped to `[0, documentHeight - viewport.height]` like in `computeCenterScroll` (lines 575–581).
  - Units: `margins.top/bottom` here are treated as **lines** (`* lineHeight`) but `ScrollMargins` type (lines 453–458) says “lines or pixels”. You should either:
    - Make them explicitly “lines” for vertical margins, or
    - Support both via a helper that normalizes to pixels.

### Wrapping algorithm

- **wrapLine** (lines 639–699) is conceptually correct for a simple word‑wrap implementation:
  - Tracks the last break opportunity.
  - Breaks at word boundaries when possible; otherwise forces a character break.
  - Skips whitespace at wrap points.
  - Gracefully handles lines with no breaks (very long words) and empty last segments.

  A few caveats worth documenting:

  - **Performance / complexity**:
    - O(n) per line with **per‑char measurement** `measureFn(char)` is realistic for monospace but expensive for proportional fonts.
    - You partly address this in the text measurement section (6.5); it’s worth cross‑linking that this function should use a batched measurement (`measureSubstring`) or layout engine in practice.

  - **Unicode / graphemes**:
    - Code indexes by JavaScript string index (`line[i]`) which is UTF‑16 code units; it will split grapheme clusters (emoji, combining marks) and surrogate pairs.
    - You already allude to grapheme vs character in `Cursor.column` (line 210). I’d explicitly note: “In real code, `i` is a grapheme index, not a raw UTF‑16 index; use a grapheme iterator.”

  - **Tabs & variable widths**:
    - You have a dedicated `measureTab` (lines 2301–2305); consider referencing it as part of `measureFn` / `measureSubstring` for correctness.

- **Empty line handling** (10.3, lines 2257–2265) is correct and necessary.

### Visual index building

- **buildVisualIndex** (lines 803–838)
  Logic is good, but a couple of minor technical gaps:

  - `lineHeight` is free‑floating; it’s not passed as a parameter. In a real implementation, this must either be:
    - A field on the editor / visual index, or
    - A parameter to `buildVisualIndex`.
  - As noted above, you ignore any per‑segment `height` (variable line heights, inline widgets).

### Cursor movement with wrapping

- **moveCursorUp / moveCursorDown** (lines 887–948) are structurally correct:
  - Uses a visual locator to map document position to `(documentLine, segmentIndex)`.
  - Traverses segments within line, then across document lines, while using `hitTestColumn` with a `preferredX` concept.

  Two things to fix/clarify:

  - As mentioned in §2: `preferredColumn` vs pixel X mismatch (lines 891–895, 923–925). You should standardize semantics.

  - For proportional fonts:
    - `computeXOffset(cursor.column, currentSegment)` should be clearly defined as using a **grapheme‑aware substring**, not raw array slicing.
    - `hitTestColumn` is already described as binary searching with measurements (lines 977–999); that’s appropriate.

### Hit‑testing

- **hitTest** / `hitTestColumn` (lines 953–1002) are conceptually good:
  - Transform viewport → content → visual line → segment → local X → column.
  - Provide a monospace and proportional path.

  But:

  - `measureSubstring(segment.startColumn, mid)` in `hitTestColumn` is missing the string or line context; earlier you used `measureSubstring(line, start, end)` in `wrapLine` (line 351). For consistency:
    ```js
    const midX = measureSubstring(segment.lineText, segment.startColumn, mid)
    ```
  - It’s worth noting that in a real impl, you’ll want to **reuse measurement data from wrapping** rather than re‑measuring inside `hitTestColumn` on every click.

### Selection rendering

- **renderSelection** (lines 1034–1089) is broadly correct:
  - It walks visible visual lines, intersects with the normalized selection range, computes `x1/x2`, and builds rectangular regions for painting.

  A few nits:

  - Uses `selection.start` / `selection.end` despite the struct using `anchor` / `head` (see §2).
  - References `viewportWidth` and `lineHeight` as globals; in a real editor those are typically fields of the renderer/viewport and should be parameters or captured state.
  - For “extend to end of line” logic (lines 1070–1081), it’s good that you restrict to the last segment of a line. That matches typical editors.

### Position adjustment on edits

- **adjustPositionForEdit** (lines 1479–1532)
  You correctly handle:

  - Position before edit → unchanged.
  - Position within replaced region → maps to the end of the inserted text (single or multi‑line).
  - Position after edit → adjust line and column by deltas.

  The math for both single‑line and multi‑line replacements is sound. The only thing missing is an explicit definition of range semantics:

  - You treat positions with `position.column <= editEnd.column` on `editEnd.line` as “inside the edit” (line 1496–1497). That is a **closed end** model; many APIs prefer half‑open `[start, end)` semantics.
  - That’s fine, but it should be explicitly documented so other algorithms (e.g., token cache invalidation) can align.

---

## 4. Code examples: correctness issues

Most examples are clearly labeled as conceptual pseudocode and are logically fine. A few concrete corrections/clarifications:

1. **Viewport visible lines (Chapter 4)**
   - Fix `lastVisibleLine` and `getVisibleDocumentLines().last` as discussed in §1.

2. **buildVisualIndex** (lines 803–838)
   - Add `lineHeight` parameter or reference a member field; right now it’s a hidden dependency.

3. **Selection type vs usage**
   - Update either the `Selection` struct (line 223) or the selection code (around 1041–1055) to use the same field names (`anchor/head` vs `start/end`).

4. **positionPopup signature** (line 1219, 1399–1404, 1462–1469)
   - Remove or rename unused third parameter (`cursor`), or use it as an anchor viewport position.

5. **Unit comments vs usage**
   - `ScrollMargins` (lines 453–458) say “lines or pixels” but `computeMinimalScroll` treats them explicitly as **lines** (multiplies by `lineHeight`). Either:
     - Change comment to “lines (vertical) / columns (horizontal)”, or
     - Add a conversion helper.

6. **MomentumScroller.applyOverscroll** (lines 2117–2137)
   - `animateMomentum` calls `this.applyOverscroll(this.scrollOffsetY)`; `applyOverscroll` is defined as a free function (lines 2074–2088). This is minor (pseudocode), but you may want to either:
     - Make `applyOverscroll` a method, or
     - Use the free function consistently.

7. **positionPopup safe area constants**
   - `scrollbarWidth`, `statusBarHeight`, `viewport.padding` are used but never defined. As a doc, that’s acceptable, but I’d add a short note that these come from editor configuration.

---

## 5. Terminology consistency and accuracy

Overall, terminology is excellent and matches real‑world editors (Monaco, JetBrains, etc.). A few inconsistencies:

- **Cursor.preferredColumn** vs “preferredColumn stores pixel X” (lines 209–213 vs 891–895). See §2.

- **Selection fields** (`anchor/head` vs `start/end`, scattered usage). See §2 and §4.

- **Scroll margins units**: “lines or pixels” vs being used as lines in `computeMinimalScroll` and `isInSafeZone` (lines 2165–2171). Decide on the canonical unit per axis and be explicit.

- **“Gutter”** is used correctly both for editor gutter and scrollbar track; the naming table in Chapter 11 (lines 2421–2437) matches common usage.

- **Visual vs document lines** are described clearly in Chapter 6 and the naming appendix; this is a strong point.

---

## 6. Missing topics / incomplete coverage

Given the scope, coverage is very good, but these are the most notable missing or thin areas:

1. **Folding / code folding + wrapping integration**
   - You define `foldedRanges` (line 196) but never:
     - Show how they affect `VisualLineIndex` / `visualLineOffsets`.
     - Adjust scrollable height when lines are folded.
     - Handle hit‑testing and cursor movement across folded regions.
   - At least a short subsection like “6.x Wrapping + Folding” would complete the picture.

2. **IME/composition and bidirectional caret**
   - You mention a hidden textarea for IME input in the hybrid rendering diagram (lines 2037–2038), but:
     - There’s no mention of **composition underlines / candidate windows** and how their anchors are mapped to document positions.
     - BiDi is briefly acknowledged (lines 2310–2318), but there’s no description of:
       - Logical vs visual cursor movement,
       - Mapping from logical index to visual caret positions,
       - Interaction with hit‑testing and selection across direction boundaries.

3. **Inline widgets / variable line height**
   - You hint at `height` on `VisualLineSegment`, but there’s no discussion of:
     - Inline decorations that change line height (code lenses, inline errors),
     - How those interact with `visualLineOffsets` and scrollable height.

4. **High‑DPI / subpixel rendering**
   - You conceptually assume integer `lineHeight` and `charWidth`. Real editors often:
     - Render at device pixels (scaled by devicePixelRatio).
     - Use fractional scroll offsets / transforms (`translate3d`, etc.).
   - A brief note that your formulas operate in **logical pixels** and may need scaling would help advanced readers.

5. **Minimap geometry**
   - You mention minimap in the pipeline (line 1895–1896) but don’t cover how minimap scroll positions map to content coordinates. This is optional, but a short sketch would make the “complete render pipeline” closer to complete.

6. **Asynchronous layout / reflow**
   - Some environments (DOM) have layout cycles and dynamic font metrics; you partially address caching, but not:
     - How to update line heights/metrics when fonts load late.
     - How to avoid layout thrash.

None of these are “must fix” for correctness, but they’re the main conceptual blind spots relative to a production‑grade editor.

---

## 7. Best practices & patterns

Generally the patterns are modern and sensible. A few notes:

- **Per‑frame full re‑render vs incremental** (Chapter 9 – immediate vs retained):
  - Guidance is good. You might add that for large documents with canvas rendering, **full re‑render per frame is usually fine** if you only render visible lines (which you already do via `visibleRange`).

- **DOM example using `innerHTML`** (lines 2007–2013):
  - Probably acceptable as pseudocode, but for best practice you might prefer:
    ```js
    element.replaceChildren(buildTokensDOM(line))
    ```
    or `textContent` when just text, to avoid HTML injection concerns.

- **Token rendering (lines 1794–1830)**:
  - You call `measureText` twice per token (once for background width, once for text). Performance tip:
    - Measure once and reuse, or precompute widths during tokenization / layout.
  - This is not “wrong” but worth noting as a perf consideration.

- **Scroll anchoring** (lines 2101–2108):
  - Modern browsers have a **scroll anchoring** concept; your description matches it. Good to maybe mention “analogous to browser scroll anchoring”.

- **Virtual scrolling** (lines 2325–2372):
  - Pattern matches typical implementations. You might briefly mention:
    - Handling variable line heights (requires recomputing transformY per line, not `lineNumber * lineHeight`).
    - Handling soft wrapping (need `visualLineIndex`, not raw `lineNumber`).

---

## 8. When to consider more advanced designs

If you revisit this doc later, triggers for a more sophisticated approach would be:

- Needing **full Unicode correctness** (graphemes, BiDi, UAX #14 line breaks) – line breaking, cursor movement, and hit‑testing all need to be built on top of a shaping engine.
- Supporting **inline widgets** and non‑uniform line heights – imply reworking `visualLineOffsets` and most scroll math.
- Implementing **editor as an embedded component** with multiple nested scrollable containers and sticky headers – requires a more explicit multi‑coordinate‑system model and scroll event propagation rules.
- Adding **code lenses / inline hints** that affect caret positions – you must decide whether they are part of the text flow or purely visual overlays.

---

If you’d like, I can provide a diff‑style patch list focusing only on the concrete fixes (off‑by‑ones, field renames, and added guard conditions) suitable for updating this doc with minimal churn.

Mathematical Formula Issues\n\n### 1.1 Scrollable Extent (lines 50-53)\n**Problem:** Can produce negative values when `contentHeight <= viewportHeight`  \n**Fix:**\n```js\nscrollableHeight = Math.max(0, contentHeight - viewportHeight)\n```\n\n### 1.2 Scroll ↔ Thumb Position (lines 76-80)\n**Problem:** Division by zero when `scrollableExtent == 0` or `trackLength == thumbLength`  \n**Fix:** Add guard: for `scrollableExtent <= 0`, fix `scrollOffset = 0`, `thumbPosition = 0`\n\n### 1.3 Visible Line Calculation (lines 422-428)\n**Problem:** Off-by-one error—`lastVisibleLine` points to line *below* viewport  \n**Fix:** Either:\n```js\nlastVisibleLine = floor((scrollOffsetY + viewportHeight - 1) / lineHeight)  // inclusive\n// OR name it:\nlastVisibleLineExclusive = ceil((scrollOffsetY + viewportHeight) / lineHeight)\n```\n\n### 1.4 getVisibleDocumentLines() (lines 849-853)\n**Problem:** Same off-by-one; `last` is exclusive but named as if inclusive  \n**Fix:** Rename to `firstInclusive` / `lastExclusive` or adjust formula\n\n### 1.5 computeLineNumberWidth (lines 371-375)\n**Problem:** `Math.log10(0)` = `-∞` when `lineCount <= 0`  \n**Fix:**\n```js\nconst safeLineCount = Math.max(1, lineCount)\nconst digitCount = Math.floor(Math.log10(safeLineCount)) + 1\n```\n\n---\n\n## 2. Data Structure Issues\n\n### 2.1 preferredColumn Semantic Mismatch\n**Location:** Definition (lines 209-213) vs usage (lines 891-895)  \n**Problem:** Type describes it as \"column\" but code treats it as **pixel X**  \n**Fix:** Rename to `preferredX` or `preferredVisualX` everywhere, or store logical column consistently\n\n### 2.2 Selection Field Inconsistency\n**Location:** Struct (lines 223-227) vs usage (lines 1050-1054, 2236-2239)  \n**Problem:** Struct uses `anchor/head`, code uses `start/end`  \n**Fix:** Standardize on one representation throughout\n\n### 2.3 Document.lineOffsets (lines 172-175)\n**Problem:** \"byte offset\" is ambiguous for UTF-8 vs UTF-16  \n**Fix:** Clarify as \"buffer offset (code units)\" or distinguish file vs buffer offsets\n\n### 2.4 VisualLineSegment.height Ignored\n**Problem:** Field defined but `buildVisualIndex` always uses constant `lineHeight`  \n**Fix:** Either remove the field or use `segment.height` for variable line heights\n\n---\n\n## 3. Algorithm Issues\n\n### 3.1 computeMinimalScroll (lines 555-569)\n**Problem:** Result not clamped to `[0, documentHeight - viewport.height]`  \n**Fix:** Add clamping like in `computeCenterScroll`\n\n### 3.2 ScrollMargins Units\n**Problem:** Type says \"lines or pixels\" but code treats as lines (multiplies by `lineHeight`)  \n**Fix:** Be explicit: \"lines (vertical) / columns (horizontal)\"\n\n### 3.3 buildVisualIndex (lines 803-838)\n**Problem:** `lineHeight` is a free-floating global, not a parameter  \n**Fix:** Pass as parameter or reference as field\n\n### 3.4 hitTestColumn measureSubstring\n**Problem:** Missing line context in call: `measureSubstring(segment.startColumn, mid)`  \n**Fix:** Should be `measureSubstring(segment.lineText, segment.startColumn, mid)`\n\n---\n\n## 4. Code Example Issues\n\n| Location | Issue | Fix |\n|----------|-------|-----|\n| positionPopup (line 1219) | Unused `cursor` parameter | Remove or use as anchor position |\n| applyOverscroll (lines 2117-2137) | Called as method but defined as free function | Make consistent |\n| DOM innerHTML (lines 2007-2013) | Potential injection concern | Use `replaceChildren()` or `textContent` |\n\n---\n\n## 5. Missing Topics\n\n### 5.1 Folding + Wrapping Integration\n- `foldedRanges` defined but never integrated with `VisualLineIndex`\n- No coverage of hit-testing/cursor movement across folded regions\n- Scrollable height adjustment for folded lines not shown\n\n### 5.2 IME/Composition\n- Hidden textarea mentioned but no composition underline/candidate window positioning\n- No caret positioning during composition\n\n### 5.3 Bidirectional Text (BiDi)\n- Briefly acknowledged but missing:\n  - Logical vs visual cursor movement\n  - Visual caret positions from logical indices\n  - Selection across direction boundaries\n\n### 5.4 Inline Widgets / Variable Line Height\n- Per-segment height field exists but no integration shown\n- Code lenses, inline errors not covered\n\n### 5.5 High-DPI / Subpixel\n- Formulas assume integer pixels\n- No mention of devicePixelRatio scaling\n\n### 5.6 Minimap Geometry\n- Mentioned in render pipeline but scroll mapping not covered\n\n---\n\n## 6. Terminology Recommendations\n\n| Current | Recommendation |\n|---------|----------------|\n| `preferredColumn` | `preferredX` or `preferredVisualX` (if pixel-based) |\n| `Selection.anchor/head` | Use consistently; don't mix with `start/end` |\n| `last` visible line | `lastInclusive` or `lastExclusive` to clarify |\n| `lineOffsets` (byte) | `bufferOffsets` (code units) |\n\n---\n\n## 7. Priority Fixes\n\n### High Priority (correctness)\n1. Fix off-by-one in visible line calculations\n2. Add division-by-zero guards for scroll formulas\n3. Guard `log10(0)` in line number width\n\n### Medium Priority (consistency)\n4. Standardize `preferredColumn` semantics\n5. Standardize `Selection` field names\n6. Add clamping to `computeMinimalScroll`\n\n### Low Priority (completeness)\n7. Add folding + wrapping integration section\n8. Document variable line height handling\n9. Add BiDi/IME notes\n\n---\n\n## Conclusion\n\nThe document is a solid reference for text editor implementation. The issues identified are mostly edge cases and naming inconsistencies rather than fundamental design flaws. Addressing the high-priority items would make the mathematical formulas production-ready.\n"
}

[Foundational Concepts: The Scrollable Region](#chapter-1-foundational-concepts-the-scrollable-region)\n11: 2. [Structural Hierarchy: Tabs, Splits, and Editor Groups](#chapter-2-structural-hierarchy-tabs-splits-and-editor-groups)\n12: 3. [The Gutter System](#chapter-3-the-gutter-system)\n13: 4. [Viewport Geometry and Line Calculations](#chapter-4-viewport-geometry-and-line-calculations)\n14: 5. [Cursor-Viewport Interaction Model](#chapter-5-cursor-viewport-interaction-model)\n15: 6. [The Complete Render Pipeline](#chapter-9-the-complete-render-pipeline)\n19: 10. [Edge Cases and Special Considerations](#chapter-10-edge-cases-and-special-considerations)\n20: 11. [Naming Conventions and Terminology](#chapter-11-naming-conventions-and-terminology)\n21: \n22: ---\n23: \n24: ## Chapter 1: Foundational Concepts: The Scrollable Region\n25: \n26: ### 1.1 Core Entities\n27: \n28: At the most fundamental level, a scrollable region consists of these conceptual components:\n29: \n30: #### Content (Document / Scrollable Content)\n31: \n32: The full extent of what *could* be displayed. Has its own coordinate system, often called **content coordinates** or *\n33: *document coordinates**. For a text editor, this might be 50,000 pixels tall even though only 800 pixels are visible at\n34: any moment.\n35: \n36: #### Viewport (View / Visible Region)\n37: \n38: The rectangular \"window\" through which you see a portion of the content. Has a fixed size (width × height) and its own\n39: coordinate system where (0,0) is typically the top-left corner of what's currently visible.\n40: \n41: #### Scroll Offset (Scroll Position)\n42: \n43: The translation between content coordinates and viewport coordinates. Typically expressed as `(scrollX, scrollY)` — how\n44: far into the content the viewport's top-left corner is positioned.\n45: \n46: #### Scrollable Extent (Scroll Range / Scroll Bounds)\n47: \n48: The maximum distance the content can scroll:\n49: \n50: ```\n51: scrollableHeight = contentHeight - viewportHeight\n52: scrollableWidth  = contentWidth - viewportWidth\n53: ```\n54: \n55: ### 1.2 The Scrollbar Anatomy\n56: \n57: The **scrollbar** is the entire control assembly, typically containing:\n58: \n59: | Part           | Alternative Names                | Purpose                                          |\n60: |----------------|----------------------------------|--------------------------------------------------|\n61: | **Track**      | Gutter, Trough, Lane             | The full channel the thumb travels along         |\n62: | **Thumb**      | Handle, Knob, Scrubber, Elevator | The draggable indicator of current position      |\n63: | **Buttons**    | Arrows, Steppers                 | Optional increment/decrement buttons at ends     |\n64: | **Page Zones** | Gutter regions                   | Clickable areas above/below thumb for page jumps |\n65: \n66: ### 1.3 Key Mathematical Relationships\n67: \n68: #### Thumb Size (Proportional Scrollbars)\n69: \n70: ```\n71: thumbLength / trackLength = viewportSize / contentSize\n72: ```\n73: \n74: The thumb visually represents what fraction of content is visible.\n75: \n76: #### Scroll Position ↔ Thumb Position\n77: \n78: ```\n79: scrollOffset / scrollableExtent = thumbPosition / (trackLength - thumbLength)\n80: ```\n81: \n82: #### Content-to-Viewport Transformation\n83: \n84: ```\n85: viewportY = contentY - scrollOffsetY\n86: contentY  = viewportY + scrollOffsetY\n87: ```\n88: \n89: ### 1.4 Coordinate Systems\n90: \n91: When implementing a scrollable region, you're juggling multiple coordinate systems:\n92: \n93: 1. **Content/Document coordinates** — Position within the full scrollable content\n94: 2. **Viewport/Client coordinates** — Position within the visible rectangle\n95: 3. **Screen/Absolute coordinates** — Position on the physical display\n96: 4. **Scrollbar-local coordinates** — For hit-testing thumb, track, and buttons\n97: \n98: ### 1.5 Visual Model\n99: \n100: ```\n101: ┌─────────────────────────────────────────┐\n102: │            Content (Document)           │\n103: │  ┌───────────────────────────────────┐  │\n104: │  │                                   │  │\n105: │  │     (content above viewport)      │  │\n106: │  │                                   │  │\n107: │  ├───────────────────────────────────┤◄─┼── scrollOffsetY\n108: │  │ ┌───────────────────────────────┐ │  │\n109: │  │ │                               │ │  │   ┌─────┐\n110: │  │ │         VIEWPORT              │ │  │   │ ▲   │\n111: │  │ │      (visible region)         │ │  │   ├─────┤\n112: │  │ │                               │ │  │   │ ░░░ │◄─ thumb\n113: │  │ │                               │ │  │   │ ░░░ │\n114: │  │ └───────────────────────────────┘ │  │   ├─────┤\n115: │  │     (content below viewport)      │  │   │     │◄─ track\n116: │  │                                   │  │   │     │\n117: │  └───────────────────────────────────┘  │   │ ▼   │\n118: │                                         │   └─────┘\n119: └─────────────────────────────────────────┘\n120: ```\n121: \n122: ---\n123: \n124: ## Chapter 2: Structural Hierarchy: Tabs, Splits, and Editor Groups\n125: \n126: Modern editors like VS Code, JetBrains IDEs, and Fleet use a hierarchical layout system for managing multiple files and\n127: views.\n128: \n129: ### 2.1 High-Level Structure\n130: \n131: ```\n132: ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n133: │ Window                                                                  │\n134: │ ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐ │\n135: │ │ Editor Area                                                         │ │\n136: │ │ ┌─────────────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────┐ │ │\n137: │ │ │ Editor Group (Left)             │ Editor Group (Right)          │ │ │\n138: │ │ │ ┌─────────────────────────────┐ │ ┌───────────────────────────┐ │ │ │\n139: │ │ │ │ Tab Bar                     │ │ │ Tab Bar                   │ │ │ │\n140: │ │ │ │ [file1.ts][file2.ts]        │ │ │ [file3.ts]                │ │ │ │\n141: │ │ │ └─────────────────────────────┘ │ └───────────────────────────┘ │ │ │\n142: │ │ │ ┌─────────────────────────────┐ │ ┌───────────────────────────┐ │ │ │\n143: │ │ │ │ Editor Pane                 │ │ │ Editor Pane               │ │ │ │\n144: │ │ │ │ ┌──────┬──────────────────┐ │ │ │                           │ │ │ │\n145: │ │ │ │ │Gutter│ Text Area        │ │ │ │                           │ │ │ │\n146: │ │ │ │ │      │                  │ │ │ │                           │ │ │ │\n147: │ │ │ │ │  1   │ const foo = ...  │▓│ │ │                           │ │ │ │\n148: │ │ │ │ │  2   │ function bar()   │▓│ │ │                           │ │ │ │\n149: │ │ │ │ │  3   │   return x;      │ │ │ │                           │ │ │ │\n150: │ │ │ │ │      │                  │ │ │ │                           │ │ │ │\n151: │ │ │ │ └──────┴──────────────────┘ │ │ │                           │ │ │ │\n152: │ │ │ └─────────────────────────────┘ │ └───────────────────────────┘ │ │ │\n153: │ │ └─────────────────────────────────┴───────────────────────────────┘ │ │\n154: │ └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘ │\n155: └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n156: ```\n157: \n158: ### 2.2 Entity Definitions\n159: \n160: #### Document / Buffer\n161: \n162: The underlying text content, independent of how it's displayed:\n163: \n164: ```\n165: Document {\n166:     content: Text               // rope, piece table, gap buffer, etc.\n167:     uri: Identifier             // file path or unique identifier\n168:     language: LanguageId        // for syntax highlighting\n169:     version: Number             // increments on edit\n170:     lineCount: Number\n171:     \n172:     // Computed/cached\n173:     lineLengths: Array<Number>\n174:     lineOffsets: Array<Number>  // byte offset where each line starts\n175: }\n176: ```\n177: \n178: A document knows nothing about viewports, cursors, or scroll positions. It is purely the data model.\n179: \n180: #### Editor State / View State\n181: \n182: Per-view state for a document. Multiple views can share one document:\n183: \n184: ```\n185: EditorState {\n186:     document: Document\n187:     \n188:     // Cursor & Selection\n189:     cursors: Array<Cursor>          // multi-cursor support\n190:     selections: Array<Selection>    // each cursor may have a selection\n191:     \n192:     // Scroll\n193:     scrollOffset: { x: Number, y: Number }\n194:     \n195:     // View-specific\n196:     foldedRanges: Array<Range>\n197:     softWrapping: Boolean\n198:     \n199:     // Behavioral state\n200:     scrollMode: 'cursor-locked' | 'free-browse'\n201:     lastScrollSource: 'user' | 'programmatic'\n202: }\n203: ```\n204: \n205: #### Cursor\n206: \n207: ```\n208: Cursor {\n209:     line: Number            // 0 or 1-indexed depending on convention\n210:     column: Number          // character offset, or grapheme cluster offset\n211:     affinity: 'left' | 'right'  // which side of a character it's \"attached\" to\n212:     preferredColumn: Number     // remembered column for vertical movement\n213: }\n214: ```\n215: \n216: The `preferredColumn` is crucial: when moving vertically through lines of varying length, the cursor should try to\n217: maintain its horizontal position. If you're at column 50, move down to a line with only 30 characters, then move down\n218: again to a line with 100 characters, the cursor should return to column 50 (or as close as possible).\n219: \n220: #### Selection\n221: \n222: ```\n223: Selection {\n224:     anchor: Position    // where selection started\n225:     head: Position      // where cursor is (selection direction)\n226:     // Alternatively: start/end with a direction flag\n227: }\n228: ```\n229: \n230: The distinction between anchor and head matters for selection direction and for knowing which end moves when extending a\n231: selection.\n232: \n233: #### Editor Group / Pane Container\n234: \n235: A container that holds a tab bar and one editor pane. Can be split:\n236: \n237: ```\n238: EditorGroup {\n239:     id: Identifier\n240:     tabs: Array<Tab>\n241:     activeTabIndex: Number\n242:     editorPane: EditorPane\n243: }\n244: \n245: Tab {\n246:     id: Identifier\n247:     label: String\n248:     editorState: EditorState    // the view state for this tab\n249:     isDirty: Boolean            // unsaved changes indicator\n250:     isPinned: Boolean           // pinned tabs don't get replaced\n251:     isPreview: Boolean          // preview mode - gets replaced on next open\n252: }\n253: ```\n254: \n255: ### 2.3 Layout Tree\n256: \n257: Splits create a tree structure:\n258: \n259: ```\n260: LayoutNode = EditorGroup | SplitContainer\n261: \n262: SplitContainer {\n263:     direction: 'horizontal' | 'vertical'\n264:     children: Array<LayoutNode>\n265:     sizes: Array<Number>        // proportions or pixel sizes\n266:     \n267:     // Resize handles (splitter bars)\n268:     splitters: Array<Splitter>\n269: }\n270: \n271: Splitter {\n272:     position: Number\n273:     minPosition: Number\n274:     maxPosition: Number\n275: }\n276: ```\n277: \n278: This recursive structure allows for arbitrary nesting of splits. A horizontal split contains children arranged\n279: left-to-right; a vertical split arranges children top-to-bottom.\n280: \n281: ### 2.4 Multi-View Document Synchronization\n282: \n283: When the same document is open in multiple views (split or tabs):\n284: \n285: ```\n286: Document changes    ──────►  All views notified\n287:                                     │\n288:                     ┌───────────────┼───────────────┐\n289:                     ▼               ▼               ▼\n290:                  View A          View B          View C\n291:                  \n292: Each view independently decides:\n293: - How to adjust scroll (if edit was in another view)\n294: - Whether to reveal cursor (if cursor moved)\n295: - How to re-render affected lines\n296: ```\n297: \n298: Key considerations:\n299: \n300: - Edits in View A that affect lines visible in View B must trigger re-render in B\n301: - If View A inserts lines above View B's viewport, should View B scroll to maintain visual stability? (This is the *\n302:   *scroll anchoring** problem)\n303: - Cursor positions are per-view, but document content is shared\n304: \n305: ---\n306: \n307: ## Chapter 3: The Gutter System\n308: \n309: The gutter is the vertical strip to the left of the text area, containing line numbers and various indicators.\n310: \n311: ### 3.1 Gutter Anatomy\n312: \n313: The gutter is a composite of multiple **gutter lanes** (or **gutter columns**):\n314: \n315: ```\n316: ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n317: │ Gutter                                  │ Text Area          │\n318: │ ┌────────┬─────┬──────┬───────────────┐ │                    │\n319: │ │Fold    │Break│ Line │ Git           │ │                    │\n320: │ │Markers │point│ Nums │ Annotations   │ │                    │\n321: │ ├────────┼─────┼──────┼───────────────┤ │                    │\n322: │ │  ▶     │  ●  │  42  │ ┃ (modified)  │ │ function foo() {   │\n323: │ │  ▼     │     │  43  │ ┃             │ │   // expanded...   │\n324: │ │        │     │  44  │               │ │   return bar;      │\n325: │ │  ▶     │  ●  │  55  │ ┃ (added)     │ │ }                  │\n326: │ └────────┴─────┴──────┴───────────────┘ │                    │\n327: └─────────────────────────────────────────┴────────────────────┘\n328: ```\n329: \n330: ### 3.2 Data Structures\n331: \n332: ```\n333: Gutter {\n334:     lanes: Array<GutterLane>\n335:     totalWidth: Number  // sum of lane widths\n336: }\n337: \n338: GutterLane {\n339:     id: String          // 'line-numbers', 'fold-markers', 'breakpoints', etc.\n340:     width: Number       // fixed or dynamic\n341:     alignment: 'left' | 'right' | 'center'\n342:     \n343:     render(line: Number, state: EditorState): GutterDecoration\n344: }\n345: \n346: GutterDecoration {\n347:     content: String | Icon      // what to display\n348:     style: GutterStyle          // colors, fonts, etc.\n349:     onClick: Function | null    // click handler (e.g., toggle breakpoint)\n350:     onHover: Function | null    // hover handler (e.g., show tooltip)\n351: }\n352: ```\n353: \n354: ### 3.3 Key Property\n355: \n356: **The gutter scrolls vertically *with* content, but is horizontally fixed.** It occupies a separate horizontal region\n357: from the text area but shares the vertical scroll offset.\n358: \n359: This means:\n360: \n361: - When you scroll the text vertically, the gutter scrolls with it\n362: - When you scroll the text horizontally, the gutter stays put\n363: - The gutter's Y coordinate system is identical to the text area's\n364: - The gutter's X coordinate system is independent\n365: \n366: ### 3.4 Dynamic Line Number Width\n367: \n368: Line number width should adapt to the document size:\n369: \n370: ```\n371: function computeLineNumberWidth(lineCount: Number, charWidth: Number): Number {\n372:     const digitCount = Math.floor(Math.log10(lineCount)) + 1\n373:     const minDigits = 2  // always show at least 2 digits worth of space\n374:     return Math.max(digitCount, minDigits) * charWidth + padding\n375: }\n376: ```\n377: \n378: When the document grows from 99 to 100 lines, the gutter needs to widen. This can cause a layout shift, so some editors\n379: pre-allocate extra space or animate the transition.\n380: \n381: ---\n382: \n383: ## Chapter 4: Viewport Geometry and Line Calculations\n384: \n385: ### 4.1 Editor Pane Structure\n386: \n387: ```\n388: EditorPane {\n389:     // Outer bounds (allocated space)\n390:     bounds: Rectangle { x, y, width, height }\n391:     \n392:     // Gutter region (fixed horizontal, scrolls vertical)\n393:     gutterBounds: Rectangle\n394:     \n395:     // Text area (scrolls both axes)\n396:     textAreaBounds: Rectangle\n397:     \n398:     // Scrollbars\n399:     verticalScrollbar: Scrollbar\n400:     horizontalScrollbar: Scrollbar\n401:     \n402:     // Computed metrics\n403:     viewportLines: {\n404:         firstVisibleLine: Number\n405:         lastVisibleLine: Number\n406:         firstFullyVisibleLine: Number\n407:         lastFullyVisibleLine: Number\n408:         partialTopLine: Number      // pixels of line clipped above viewport\n409:         partialBottomLine: Number   // pixels of line clipped below viewport\n410:     }\n411:     \n412:     lineHeight: Number\n413:     charWidth: Number   // for monospace; variable-width is more complex\n414: }\n415: ```\n416: \n417: ### 4.2 Viewport Line Calculation\n418: \n419: For a simple (non-wrapped) editor:\n420: \n421: ```\n422: firstVisibleLine = floor(scrollOffsetY / lineHeight)\n423: lastVisibleLine = floor((scrollOffsetY + viewportHeight) / lineHeight)\n424: \n425: // Fractional/partial visibility\n426: topLineVisiblePortion = 1 - ((scrollOffsetY % lineHeight) / lineHeight)\n427: bottomLineVisiblePortion = ((scrollOffsetY + viewportHeight) % lineHeight) / lineHeight\n428: ```\n429: \n430: ### 4.3 Fully vs Partially Visible Lines\n431: \n432: ```\n433: firstFullyVisibleLine = ceil(scrollOffsetY / lineHeight)\n434: lastFullyVisibleLine = floor((scrollOffsetY + viewportHeight) / lineHeight) - 1\n435: \n436: // Or more precisely:\n437: lastFullyVisibleLine = floor((scrollOffsetY + viewportHeight - lineHeight) / lineHeight)\n438: ```\n439: \n440: The distinction matters for operations like \"page down\" which might want to keep the last fully visible line as the new\n441: first line.\n442: \n443: ---\n444: \n445: ## Chapter 5: Cursor-Viewport Interaction Model\n446: \n447: This is where text editors become genuinely complex. The relationship between cursor position and scroll position has\n448: many nuances.\n449: \n450: ### 5.1 Scroll Margins / Keep-in-View Zone\n451: \n452: ```\n453: ScrollMargins {\n454:     top: Number         // lines or pixels\n455:     bottom: Number\n456:     left: Number        // columns or pixels  \n457:     right: Number\n458: }\n459: ```\n460: \n461: The **safe zone** is the viewport minus these margins. The cursor should stay within the safe zone during normal\n462: editing:\n463: \n464: ```\n465: ┌─────────────────────────────────────────┐\n466: │ ░░░░░░░░░░ top margin (3 lines) ░░░░░░░ │\n467: ├─────────────────────────────────────────┤\n468: │                                         │\n469: │           SAFE ZONE                     │\n470: │     (cursor can be here freely)         │\n471: │                                         │\n472: ├─────────────────────────────────────────┤\n473: │ ░░░░░░░░ bottom margin (5 lines) ░░░░░░ │\n474: └─────────────────────────────────────────┘\n475: ```\n476: \n477: ### 5.2 Scroll Modes / States\n478: \n479: ```\n480: enum ScrollMode {\n481:     CURSOR_LOCKED,      // viewport follows cursor\n482:     FREE_BROWSE,        // user is exploring, cursor may be off-screen\n483:     REVEAL_PENDING      // cursor is off-screen, next edit will reveal it\n484: }\n485: ```\n486: \n487: ### 5.3 Scroll Triggers\n488: \n489: Different actions have different scroll behaviors:\n490: \n491: | Trigger                                     | Typical Behavior                                                 |\n492: |---------------------------------------------|------------------------------------------------------------------|\n493: | **Typing/editing**                          | Reveal cursor, respect margins, snap to cursor-locked mode       |\n494: | **Cursor movement (arrow keys)**            | Keep cursor in safe zone, scroll minimally                       |\n495: | **Mouse wheel**                             | Free scroll, enter free-browse mode                              |\n496: | **Scrollbar drag**                          | Free scroll, enter free-browse mode                              |\n497: | **Page Up/Down**                            | Move cursor AND viewport together                                |\n498: | **Ctrl+G (go to line)**                     | Center target line, cursor-locked mode                           |\n499: | **Click in text area**                      | Move cursor, stay at current scroll (unless clicking off-screen) |\n500: | **Search result jump**                      | Center or reveal result, cursor-locked mode                      |\n[... 1660 lines omitted ...]\n501: // Is line 10 in the safe zone if margin is 3 lines and viewport shows lines 7-20?\n502: // Lines 7,8,9 are in top margin → line 10 is at edge\n503: // Typically: treat \"at edge\" as \"inside\" to avoid jitter\n504: \n505: function isInSafeZone(cursorY, scrollOffset, viewport, margins) {\n506:     const safeTop = scrollOffset + margins.top * lineHeight\n507:     const safeBottom = scrollOffset + viewport.height - margins.bottom * lineHeight\n508:     \n509:     // Use >= and <= (inclusive) to avoid jitter at edges\n510:     return cursorY >= safeTop && cursorY + lineHeight <= safeBottom\n511: }\n512: ```\n513: \n514: #### Very Small Viewport\n515: \n516: ```\n517: // Viewport is 5 lines, margins are 3 each → impossible!\n518: // Solution: reduce margins dynamically when viewport is small\n519: \n520: function computeEffectiveMargins(configuredMargins, viewportLines) {\n521:     const maxMargin = Math.floor(viewportLines / 4)\n522:     return {\n523:         top: Math.min(configuredMargins.top, maxMargin),\n524:         bottom: Math.min(configuredMargins.bottom, maxMargin)\n525:     }\n526: }\n527: ```\n528: \n529: #### Multi-Cursor Scenarios\n530: \n531: ```\n532: // 3 cursors at lines 10, 500, 1000 → which one to follow?\n533: \n534: function determinePrimaryCursor(cursors, policy) {\n535:     switch (policy) {\n536:         case 'first':\n537:             return cursors[0]\n538:         case 'last':\n539:             return cursors[cursors.length - 1]\n540:         case 'most-recent':\n541:             return cursors.reduce((a, b) => \n542:                 a.lastModified > b.lastModified ? a : b\n543:             )\n544:         case 'any-visible':\n545:             // Prefer cursor already in view\n546:             return cursors.find(c => this.isInViewport(c)) ?? cursors[0]\n547:     }\n548: }\n549: ```\n550: \n551: #### Horizontal Scroll with Long Lines\n552: \n553: Same margin logic applies horizontally:\n554: \n555: ```\n556: function ensureCursorVisibleHorizontal(cursor) {\n557:     const cursorX = computeCursorX(cursor)\n558:     const safeLeft = scrollOffsetX + margins.left\n559:     const safeRight = scrollOffsetX + viewport.width - margins.right\n560:     \n561:     if (cursorX < safeLeft) {\n562:         scrollOffsetX = cursorX - margins.left\n563:     } else if (cursorX > safeRight) {\n564:         scrollOffsetX = cursorX - viewport.width + margins.right\n565:     }\n566: }\n567: ```\n568: \n569: #### Selection Extends Beyond Viewport\n570: \n571: ```\n572: // Selection from line 10 to line 500, viewport shows 50-70\n573: // Cursor (selection head) might be at line 500, anchor at line 10\n574: // Follow the head, not the anchor\n575: \n576: function ensureSelectionEndVisible(selection) {\n577:     // Always reveal the head (where cursor is)\n578:     ensureCursorVisible(selection.head)\n579:     \n580:     // Optionally: if selection started outside view, show indicator\n581:     if (!isInViewport(selection.anchor)) {\n582:         showSelectionExtendsIndicator(selection.anchor.line < viewport.firstLine \n583:             ? 'above' \n584:             : 'below'\n585:         )\n586:     }\n587: }\n588: ```\n589: \n590: ### 10.3 Wrapping Edge Cases\n591: \n592: #### Empty Lines\n593: \n594: Empty lines still need a visual line entry:\n595: \n596: ```\n597: function wrapLine(line) {\n598:     if (line.length === 0) {\n599:         return [{\n600:             startColumn: 0,\n601:             endColumn: 0,\n602:             width: 0\n603:         }]\n604:     }\n605:     // ... normal wrapping\n606: }\n607: ```\n608: \n609: #### Very Long Words\n610: \n611: When a single \"word\" exceeds the viewport width:\n612: \n613: ```\n614: // Word: \"supercalifragilisticexpialidocious_and_more_stuff\"\n615: // Viewport width: 200px\n616: // Word width: 400px\n617: \n618: // Options:\n619: // 1. Force character wrap (break mid-word)\n620: // 2. Allow horizontal overflow (show scrollbar)\n621: // 3. Shrink text to fit (bad for editing)\n622: \n623: // Most editors use option 1:\n624: if (lastBreakPoint === currentStart && currentWidth > maxWidth) {\n625:     // No break opportunity - force break here\n626:     segments.push({\n627:         startColumn: currentStart,\n628:         endColumn: i,\n629:         width: currentWidth - charWidth\n630:     })\n631:     currentStart = i\n632:     currentWidth = charWidth\n633: }\n634: ```\n635: \n636: #### Tab Characters\n637: \n638: Tabs have variable width based on position:\n639: \n640: ```\n641: function measureTab(column, tabSize) {\n642:     const tabStop = Math.floor(column / tabSize) + 1\n643:     const nextTabColumn = tabStop * tabSize\n644:     return (nextTabColumn - column) * charWidth\n645: }\n646: ```\n647: \n648: #### Bidirectional Text\n649: \n650: RTL text mixed with LTR requires the Unicode Bidirectional Algorithm:\n651: \n652: ```\n653: // \"Hello שלום World\"\n654: // Visual order might be: \"Hello םולש World\"\n655: // Cursor movement becomes complex - \"visual\" vs \"logical\" order\n656: ```\n657: \n658: This is a deep topic unto itself.\n659: \n660: ### 10.4 Virtual Scrolling\n661: \n662: For documents with 100k+ lines, rendering all lines is impractical:\n663: \n664: ```\n665: VirtualScroller {\n666:     // Only keep DOM/render state for visible lines + buffer\n667:     buffer: Number = 5 onScroll() {\n673:         const visibleStart = Math.floor(scrollOffsetY / lineHeight)\n674:         const visibleEnd = Math.ceil((scrollOffsetY + viewport.height) / lineHeight)\n675:         \n676: of view\n680:         for (let i = this.renderedRange.start; i < newStart; i++) {\n681:             this.recycleLine(i)\n682:         }\n683:         for (let i = newEnd; i < this.renderedRange.end; i++) {\n684:             this.recycleLine(i)\n685:         }\n686:         \n687:         // Render newly visible lines\n688:         for (let i = newStart; i < this.renderedRange.start; i++) {\n689:             this.renderLine(i)\n690:         }\n691:         for (let i = this.renderedRange.end; i < newEnd; i++) {\n692:             this.renderLine(i)\n693:         }\n694:         \n695:         this.renderedRange = { start: newStart, end: newEnd }\n696:     }\n697:     \n698:     recycleLine(lineNumber) {\n699:         const element = this.lineElements.get(lineNumber)\n700:         if (element) {\n701:             this.linePool.push(element)\n702:             this.lineElements.delete(lineNumber)\n703:         }\n704:     }\n705:     \n706:     renderLine(lineNumber) {\n707:         const element = this.linePool.pop() ?? this.createLineElement()\n708:         element.style.transform = `translateY(${lineNumber * lineHeight}px)`\n709:         element.textContent = document.getLine(lineNumber)\n710:         this.lineElements.set(lineNumber, element)\n711:     }\n712: }\n713: ```\n714: \n715: ### 10.5 Nested Scrolling\n716: \n717: When viewports are nested (editor inside a scrollable panel):\n718: \n719: ```\n720: function handleWheel(event, target) {\n721:     const scrollableAncestors = findScrollableAncestors(target)\n722:     \n723:     for (const scroller of scrollableAncestors) {\n724:         if (scroller.canScrollInDirection(event.deltaY)) {\n725:             scroller.scroll(event.deltaY)\n726:             event.stopPropagation()\n727: Terminology\n737: \n738: A reference for the various names used across different editors and frameworks.\n739: \n740: ### Document & Content\n741: \n742: | Concept                | Common Names                                  |\n743: |------------------------|-----------------------------------------------|\n744: | Full content           | Document, Buffer, Model, TextModel            |\n745: | Content data structure | Rope, Piece Table, Gap Buffer, Array of Lines |\n746: | Single line content    | Line, TextLine, LineContent                   |\n747: | Position in document   | Position, Location, Point, Offset             |\n748: | Range in document      | Range, Span, Region, Selection                |\n749: \n750: ### View & State\n751: \n752: | Concept             | Common Names                                                   |\n753: |---------------------|----------------------------------------------------------------|\n754: | View state          | EditorState, ViewState, EditorViewModel, ViewConfiguration     |\n755: | Visible area        | Viewport, View, VisibleRange, ViewRegion                       |\n756: | Scroll distance     | ScrollOffset, ScrollPosition, ScrollTop/ScrollLeft             |\n757: | Keep-in-view buffer | ScrollMargin, ScrollPadding, CursorSurroundingLines, ScrollOff |\n758: \n759: ### Layout\n760: \n761: | Concept                | Common Names                                             |\n762: |------------------------|----------------------------------------------------------|\n763: | Line number area       | Gutter, Margin, LineNumberGutter, LineDecorations        |\n764: | Sub-regions of gutter  | GutterLane, GutterColumn, GutterDecoration, MarginColumn |\n765: | Tab container          | EditorGroup, TabGroup, Pane, TabBar                      |\n766: | Split container        | SplitView, SplitContainer, EditorGrid, PaneGroup         |\n767: | Divider between splits | Splitter, Sash, ResizeHandle, Divider |----------------------|----------------------------------------------------------|\n773: | Scroll follow mode   | ScrollMode, CursorFollow, RevealMode, AutoScroll         |\n774: | Scroll reveal action | RevealCursor, EnsureVisible, ScrollIntoView, RevealRange |\n775: | Scrollbar thumb      | Thumb, Handle, Knob, Scrubber, Elevator                  |\n776: | Scrollbar track      | Track, Gutter, Trough, Lane, Channel                     |\n777: \n778: ### Wrapping\n779: \n780: | Concept           | Common Names                                      |\n781: |-------------------|---------------------------------------------------|\n782: | Soft wrap         | Word Wrap, Line Wrap, Text Wrap                   |\n783: | Wrapped line unit | VisualLine, ScreenLine, DisplayLine, PhysicalLine |\n784: | Document line     | LogicalLine, BufferLine, DocumentLine, TextLine   |\n785: | Wrap segments     | VisualLineSegment, WrapSegment, SubLine, ViewLine |\n786: \n787: ### Overlays\n788: \n789: | Concept            | Common Names                                                |\n790: |--------------------|-------------------------------------------------------------|\n791: | Autocomplete popup | CompletionWidget, IntelliSense, Autocomplete, SuggestionBox |\n792: | Hover info Squiggles, ErrorMarkers, LintMarkers           |\n795: \n796: ### Cursor & Selection\n797: \n798: | Concept          | Common Names                                |\n799: |------------------|---------------------------------------------|\n800: | Cursor           | Caret, Cursor, InsertionPoint, TextCursor   |\n801: | Selection        | Selection, Range, Highlight, SelectedText   |\n802: | Multi-cursor     | MultiCursor, MultipleSelections, MultiCaret |\n803: | Selection anchor | Anchor, SelectionStart, SelectionOrigin     |\n804: | Selection head   | Head, Cursor, ActiveEnd, SelectionEnd       |\n805: \n806: ---\n807: \n808: ## Appendix A: Event Flow Example\n809: \n810: User presses Down Arrow while cursor is at bottom margin:\n811: \n812: ```\n813: 1.  KeyDown event captured by editor\n814: 2.  Command resolver maps key to action: \"cursorDown\"\n815: 3.  Command dispatcher invokes cursor controller\n816: 4.  Cursor position updated: line 45 → line 46\n817: 5.  EditorState emits 'cursor-changed' event\n818: 6.  Scroll controller receives event, checks cursor vs safe zone:\n819:     - Current safe zone bottom = scrollY + viewportHeight - marginBottom\n820:     - Cursor Y = line 46 * lineHeight\n821:     - Cursor below safe zone? Yes → compute new scrollY\n822: 7.  If scroll needed:\n823:     - scrollOffset.y updated to reveal cursor\n824:     - Scrollbar controller notified\n825:     - Scrollbar thumb position recalculated\n826: 8.  Viewport marks visible range dirty\n827: 9.  Render scheduled via requestAnimationFrame\n828: 10. On next frame:\n829:     - Layout phase computes new visible line range\n830:     - Gutter re-renders (new lines visible, line numbers shift)\n831:     - Text area re-renders (new lines visible)\n832:     - Cursor caret re-renders at new position\n833:     - Scrollbar thumb re-renders at new position\n834: 11. Any visible overlays repositioned (if anchor moved)\n835: ```\n836: \n837: ---\n838: \n839: ## Appendix B: Coordinate Transformation Functions\n840: \n841: ```\n842: // Document position → viewport pixel position\n843: function documentToViewport(docPos: Position): Point {\n844:     // Get visual line for this document position\n845:     const mapping = visualIndex.documentToVisual[docPos.line]\n846:     const segment = findSegmentContainingColumn(docPos.line, docPos.column)\n847:     const visualLine = mapping.firstVisualLine + segment.index\n848:     \n849:     // Compute Y from visual line offset\n850:     const y = visualIndex.visualLineOffsets[visualLine] - scrollOffsetY\n851:     \n852:     // Compute X from column within segment\n853:     const x = gutterWidth + segment.wrapIndent + \n854:               computeXOffset(docPos.column, segment) - scrollOffsetX\n855:     \n856:     return { x, y }\n857: }\n858: \n859: // Viewport pixel position → document position\n860: function viewportToDocument(viewportPos: Point): Position {\n861:     // Convert to content coordinates\n862:     const contentY = viewportPos.y + scrollOffsetY\n863:     const contentX = viewportPos.x + scrollOffsetX - gutterWidth\n864:     \n865:     // Find visual line\n866:     const visualLine = binarySearchFloor(\n867:         visualIndex.visualLineOffsets, \n868:         contentY\n869:     )\n870:     const { documentLine, segmentIndex } = visualIndex.visualToDocument[visualLine]\n871:     \n872:     // Find column\n873:     const segment = getSegment(documentLine, segmentIndex)\n874:     const localX = contentX - segment.wrapIndent\n875:     const column = hitTestColumn(segment, localX)\n876:     \n877:     return { line: documentLine, column }\n878: }\n879: \n880: // Document position → screen/absolute position\n881: function documentToScreen(docPos: Position): Point {\n882:     const viewportPos = documentToViewport(docPos)\n883:     const editorRect = editorElement.getBoundingClientRect()\n884:     return {\n885:         x: editorRect.left + viewportPos.x,\n886:         y: editorRect.top + viewportPos.y\n887:     }\n888: }\n889: \n890: // Screen/absolute position → document position\n891: function screenToDocument(screenPos: Point): Position {\n892:     const editorRect = editorElement.getBoundingClientRect()\n893:     const viewportPos = {\n894:         x: screenPos.x - editorRect.left,\n895:         y: screenPos.y - editorRect.top\n896:     }\n897:     return viewportToDocument(viewportPos)\n898: }\n899: ```\n900: \n901: ---\n902: \n903: ## Appendix C: Binary Search Helpers\n904: \n905: ```\n906: // Find largest index where array[index] <= target\n907: function binarySearchFloor(array: Array<Number>, target: Number): Number {\n908:     let low = 0\n909:     let high = array.length - 1\n910:     let result = 0\n911:     \n912:     while (low <= high) {\n913:         const mid = Math.floor((low + high) / 2)\n914:         \n915:         if (array[mid] <= target) {\n916:             result = mid\n917:             low = mid + 1\n918:         } else {\n919:             high = mid - 1\n920:         }\n921:     }\n922:     \n923:     return result\n924: }\n925: \n926: // Find smallest index where array[index] >= target\n927: function binarySearchCeil(array: Array<Number>, target: Number): Number {\n928:     let low = 0\n929:     let high = array.length - 1\n930:     let result = array.length - 1\n931:     \n932:     while (low <= high) {\n933:         const mid = Math.floor((low + high) / 2)\n934:         \n935:         if (array[mid] >= target) {\n936:             result = mid\n937:             high = mid - 1\n938:         } else {\n939:             low = mid + 1\n940:         }\n941:     }\n942:     \n943:     return result\n944: }\n945: ```\n946: \n947: ---\n948: \n949: ## Appendix D: Useful Constants and Defaults\n950: \n951: ```\n952: // Typical default values (customize per editor)\n953: \n954: const DEFAULTS = {\n955:     // Scroll margins\n956:     scrollMarginTop: 3,         // lines\n957:     scrollMarginBottom: 3,      // lines  \n958:     scrollMarginLeft: 10,       // pixels or columns\n959:     scrollMarginRight: 10,      // pixels or columns\n960:     \n961:     // Scrollbar\n962:     scrollbarMinThumbSize: 30,  // pixels\n963:     scrollbarWidth: 14,         // pixels\n964:     scrollbarTrackMargin: 2,    // pixels\n965:     \n966:     // Cursor\n967:     cursorBlinkRate: 530,       // milliseconds\n968:     cursorWidth: 2,             // pixels for line cursor\n969:     cursorSmoothCaretAnimation: true,\n970:     \n971:     // Autocomplete\n972:     autocompleteMaxItems: 12,\n973:     autocompleteItemHeight: 22, // pixels\n974:     autocompleteMinWidth: 200,  // pixels\n975:     autocompleteMaxWidth: 400,  // pixels\n976:     autocompleteGap: 2,         // pixels from cursor\n977:     \n978:     // Wrapping\n979:     wrapIndent: 'same',         // 'none' | 'same' | 'indent' | Number\n980:     wrapAtWordBoundary: true,\n981:     \n982:     // Virtual scrolling\n983:     virtualScrollBuffer: 5,     // lines above/below viewport\n984:     \n985:     // Gutter\n986:     gutterMinWidth: 40,         // pixels\n987:     gutterPadding: 8,           // pixels (each side)\n988:     \n989:     // Line height\n990:     lineHeightMultiplier: 1.5,  // relative to font size\n991:     \n992:     // Font\n993:     fontFamily: 'monospace',\n994:     fontSize: 14,               // pixels\n995: }\n996: ```\n997: \n998: ---\n999: \n1000: *End of document*",

  "content": "# Implementer's Guide to Modern Text Editor UI Geometry, Mathematics, Edge Cases, and Problems\n\nA comprehensive reference for building scrollable text editing interfaces with tabs, split panels, soft wrapping,\nautocomplete overlays, and all the coordinate transformations in between.\n\n> **Target Audience:** Desktop software developers building code editors in Rust, Go, C++, or similar languages.\n> This guide assumes monospace fonts and left-to-right text direction. Web-specific concerns (DOM, CSS) are\n> mentioned only where relevant for conceptual understanding.\n\n---\n\n## Table of Contents\n\n1. [Foundational Concepts: The Scrollable Region](#chapter-1-foundational-concepts-the-scrollable-region)\n2. [Structural Hierarchy: Tabs, Splits, and Editor Groups](#chapter-2-structural-hierarchy-tabs-splits-and-editor-groups)\n3. [The Gutter System](#chapter-3-the-gutter-system)\n4. [Viewport Geometry and Line Calculations](#chapter-4-viewport-geometry-and-line-calculations)\n5. [Cursor-Viewport Interaction Model](#chapter-5-cursor-viewport-interaction-model)\n6. [Soft Wrapping: The Coordinate System Split](#chapter-6-soft-wrapping-the-coordinate-system-split)\n7. [Autocomplete and Overlay Positioning](#chapter-7-autocomplete-and-overlay-positioning)\n8. [Cursor Styles: Pipe, Block, and Underline](#chapter-12-cursor-styles-pipe-block-and-underline)\n\n**Appendices:**\n- [A: Event Flow Example](#appendix-a-event-flow-example)\n- [B: Coordinate Transformation Functions](#appendix-b-coordinate-transformation-functions)\n- [C: Binary Search Helpers](#appendix-c-binary-search-helpers)\n- [D: Useful Constants and Defaults](#appendix-d-useful-constants-and-defaults)\n- [E: Unicode and UTF-8 Considerations for Code Editors](#appendix-e-unicode-and-utf-8-considerations-for-code-editors)\n- [F: Right-to-Left and Bidirectional Text Theory](#appendix-f-right-to-left-and-bidirectional-text-theory)\n\n---\n"

{
  "path": "~/code/rust-editor/docs_chapters/chapter01.md",
  "content": "## Chapter 1: Foundational Concepts: The Scrollable Region\n\n### 1.1 Core Entities\n\nAt the most fundamental level, a scrollable region consists of these conceptual components:\n\n#### Content (Document / Scrollable Content)\n\nThe full extent of what *could* be displayed. Has its own coordinate system, often called **content coordinates** or\n**document coordinates**. For a text editor, this might be 50,000 pixels tall even though only 800 pixels are visible at\nany moment.\n\n#### Viewport (View / Visible Region)\n\nThe rectangular \"window\" through which you see a portion of the content. Has a fixed size (width × height) and its own\ncoordinate system where (0,0) is typically the top-left corner of what's currently visible.\n\n#### Scroll Offset (Scroll Position)\n\nThe translation between content coordinates and viewport coordinates. Typically expressed as `(scrollX, scrollY)` — how\nfar into the content the viewport's top-left corner is positioned.\n\n#### Scrollable Extent (Scroll Range / Scroll Bounds)\n\nThe maximum distance the content can scroll. Always clamped to non-negative values:\n\n```\nscrollableHeight = max(0, contentHeight - viewportHeight)\nscrollableWidth  = max(0, contentWidth - viewportWidth)\n```\n\nWhen `contentHeight <= viewportHeight`, there is no vertical scroll (scrollableHeight = 0).\n\n### 1.2 The Scrollbar Anatomy\n\nThe **scrollbar** is the entire control assembly, typically containing:\n\n| Part           | Alternative Names                | Purpose                                          |\n|----------------|----------------------------------|--------------------------------------------------|\n| **Track**      | Gutter, Trough, Lane             | The full channel the thumb travels along         |\n| **Thumb**      | Handle, Knob, Scrubber, Elevator | The draggable indicator of current position      |\n| **Buttons**    | Arrows, Steppers                 | Optional increment/decrement buttons at ends     |\n| **Page Zones** | Gutter regions                   | Clickable areas above/below thumb for page jumps |\n\n### 1.3 Key Mathematical Relationships\n\n#### Thumb Size (Proportional Scrollbars)\n\n```\nthumbLength / trackLength = viewportSize / contentSize\n```\n\nThe thumb visually represents what fraction of content is visible.\n\n**Edge case:** When `contentSize <= viewportSize`, either hide the scrollbar entirely or set `thumbLength = trackLength`\n(thumb fills the track, indicating no scrolling possible).\n\n#### Scroll Position ↔ Thumb Position\n\n```\n// Only valid when scrollableExtent > 0 and trackLength > thumbLength\nscrollOffset / scrollableExtent = thumbPosition / (trackLength - thumbLength)\n```\n\n**Edge cases to handle:**\n- `scrollableExtent == 0`: No scrolling possible, fix `scrollOffset = 0`, `thumbPosition = 0`\n- `trackLength == thumbLength`: Thumb fills track, fix `thumbPosition = 0`\n\n```\nfunction scrollOffsetToThumbPosition(scrollOffset, scrollableExtent, trackLength, thumbLength) {\n    if (scrollableExtent <= 0 || trackLength <= thumbLength) {\n        return 0\n    }\n    return (scrollOffset / scrollableExtent) * (trackLength - thumbLength)\n}\n\nfunction thumbPositionToScrollOffset(thumbPosition, scrollableExtent, trackLength, thumbLength) {\n    if (scrollableExtent <= 0 || trackLength <= thumbLength) {\n        return 0\n    }\n    return (thumbPosition / (trackLength - thumbLength)) * scrollableExtent\n}\n```\n\n#### Content-to-Viewport Transformation\n\n```\nviewportY = contentY - scrollOffsetY\ncontentY  = viewportY + scrollOffsetY\n```\n\n### 1.4 Coordinate Systems\n\nWhen implementing a scrollable region, you're juggling multiple coordinate systems:\n\n1. **Content/Document coordinates** — Position within the full scrollable content (pixels)\n2. **Viewport/Client coordinates** — Position within the visible rectangle (pixels)\n3. **Screen/Absolute coordinates** — Position on the physical display (pixels)\n4. **Scrollbar-local coordinates** — For hit-testing thumb, track, and buttons (pixels)\n5. **Document coordinates** — Position within text (line number, column number)\n\n### 1.5 Visual Model\n\n```\n┌─────────────────────────────────────────┐\n│            Content (Document)           │\n│  ┌───────────────────────────────────┐  │\n│  │                                   │  │\n│  │     (content above viewport)      │  │\n│  │                                   │  │\n│  ├───────────────────────────────────┤◄─┼── scrollOffsetY\n│  │ ┌───────────────────────────────┐ │  │\n│  │ │                               │ │  │   ┌─────┐\n│  │ │         VIEWPORT              │ │  │   │ ▲   │\n│  │ │      (visible region)         │ │  │   ├─────┤\n│  │ │                               │ │  │   │ ░░░ │◄─ thumb\n│  │ │                               │ │  │   │ ░░░ │\n│  │ └───────────────────────────────┘ │  │   ├─────┤\n│  │     (content below viewport)      │  │   │     │◄─ track\n│  │                                   │  │   │     │\n│  └───────────────────────────────────┘  │   │ ▼   │\n│                                         │   └─────┘\n└─────────────────────────────────────────┘\n```\n\n---\n"
}
```
