Performance Monitoring Integration\n \n+> **Reference:** See `TODO.md` Feature 4 for the planned performance monitoring system using\n+> [perf-monitor-rs](https://github.com/larksuite/perf-monitor-rs).\n+\n+### Why Performance Monitoring?\n+\n+As the editor grows, you need visibility into:\n+\n+1. **Frame times** — Is rendering staying under 16ms (60 FPS)?\n+2. **Update latency** — Which `Msg` handlers are slow?\n+3. **Memory usage** — Are we leaking memory on large files?\n+4. **I/O performance** — How long do file operations take?\n+\n+Without instrumentation, performance regressions creep in unnoticed. The Elm architecture makes this easy because all state changes flow through `update`—a natural instrumentation point.\n+\n+### How It Fits the Architecture\n+\n+Performance monitoring is a **cross-cutting concern** that fits cleanly as a separate domain:\n+\n+```\n+┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n+│  AppModel                                                       │\n+│  ┌────────────┐ ┌──────────────┐ ┌─────────┐ ┌───────────────┐  │\n+│  │ documents  │ │ editors      │ │ ui      │ │ debug         │  │\n+│  │            │ │              │ │         │ │ (perf stats)  │  │\n+│  └────────────┘ └──────────────┘ └─────────┘ └───────────────┘  │\n+└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n+```\n+\n+### State Structure\n+\n+```rust\n+/// Debug/performance state - can be compile-gated with #[cfg(feature = \"perf\")]\n+struct DebugState {\n+    perf_enabled: bool,\n+    show_overlay: bool,\n+    \n+    // Frame timing\n+    frame_times: RingBuffer<Duration, 120>,     // Last 120 frames (~2 sec at 60fps)\n+    last_frame_start: Instant,\n+    \n+    // Per-message timing (which handlers are slow?)\n+    update_times: HashMap<String, MovingAverage>,\n+    \n+    // System metrics from perf-monitor-rs\n+    cpu_usage: Option<f64>,\n+    memory_usage: Option<MemoryInfo>,\n+    \n+    // Render stats\n+    lines_rendered: usize,\n+    glyphs_cached: usize,\n+}\n+\n+struct MemoryInfo {\n+    resident: usize,        // RSS in bytes\n+    virtual_size: usize,\n+    allocations: usize,     // If tracking with a custom allocator\n+}\n+```\n+\n+### Message Integration\n+\n+```rust\n+enum Msg {\n+    Editor(EditorId, EditorMsg),\n+    Document(DocumentId, DocumentMsg),\n+    Ui(UiMsg),\n+    App(AppMsg),\n+    Debug(DebugMsg),  // New domain for perf monitoring\n+}\n+\n+enum DebugMsg {\n+    TogglePerfOverlay,              // Keybinding: F12 or similar\n+    UpdatePerfStats(PerfSnapshot),  // Posted from background sampler\n+    RecordFrameTime(Duration),      // Posted after each render\n+    ResetStats,                     // Clear accumulated data\n+}\n+```\n+\n+### Command Integration\n+\n+The expanded `Cmd` system handles background sampling:\n+\n+```rust\n+enum Cmd {\n+    Redraw,\n+    SaveFile { document_id: DocumentId },\n+    LoadFile { path: PathBuf },\n+    \n+    // Performance monitoring commands\n+    StartPerfSampler { interval_ms: u64 },\n+    StopPerfSampler,\n+}\n+```\n+\n+### Implementation Pattern\n+\n+```rust\n+// In event loop - command processing\n+fn process_cmd(cmd: Cmd, tx: Sender<Msg>, app: &AppModel) {\n+    match cmd {\n+        Cmd::StartPerfSampler { interval_ms } => {\n+            let tx = tx.clone();\n+            std::thread::spawn(move || {\n+                loop {\n+                    std::thread::sleep(Duration::from_millis(interval_ms));\n+                    \n+                    // Collect system metrics\n+                    let snapshot = PerfSnapshot {\n+                        cpu: perf_monitor::cpu::processor_numbers().ok(),\n+                        memory: perf_monitor::mem::get_process_memory_info().ok(),\n+                        fd_count: perf_monitor::fd::fd_count_cur().ok(),\n+                    };\n+                    \n+                    // Post back to main thread\n+                    if tx.send(Msg::Debug(DebugMsg::UpdatePerfStats(snapshot))).is_err() {\n+                        break; // Channel closed, exit sampler\n+                    }\n+                }\n+            });\n+        }\n+        // ... other commands\n+    }\n+}\n+\n+// Instrumented update loop\n+fn update(app: &mut AppModel, msg: Msg) -> Option<Cmd> {\n+    let start = Instant::now();\n+    let msg_name = msg.variant_name(); // Debug representation\n+    \n+    let result = match msg {\n+        Msg::Editor(id, emsg) => update_editor(app, id, emsg),\n+        Msg::Document(id, dmsg) => update_document(app, id, dmsg),\n+        Msg::Ui(umsg) => update_ui(&mut app.ui, umsg),\n+        Msg::App(amsg) => update_app(app, amsg),\n+        Msg::Debug(dmsg) => update_debug(&mut app.debug, dmsg),\n+    };\n+    \n+    // Record timing if perf monitoring enabled\n+    if app.debug.perf_enabled {\n+        let elapsed = start.elapsed();\n+        app.debug.update_times\n+            .entry(msg_name)\n+            .or_default()\n+            .record(elapsed);\n+    }\n+    \n+    result\n+}\n+```\n+\n+### Display Options\n+\n+| Option | Description | Use Case |\n+|--------|-------------|----------|\n+| **Status bar** | FPS + memory in existing status line | Always-on lightweight |\n+| **Overlay panel** | Detailed stats overlay (F12 toggle) | Debugging sessions |\n+| **Log file** | Write metrics to stderr or file | CI / profiling |\n+| **Headless export** | JSON stats dump | Automated benchmarks |\n+\n+```rust\n+// Overlay rendering (in render loop, after main content)\n+fn render_perf_overlay(debug: &DebugState, ctx: &mut RenderContext) {\n+    if !debug.show_overlay { return; }\n+    \n+    let avg_frame_time = debug.frame_times.average();\n+    let fps = 1.0 / avg_frame_time.as_secs_f64();\n+    \n+    let lines = [\n+        format!(\"FPS: {:.1}\", fps),\n+        format!(\"Frame: {:.2}ms\", avg_frame_time.as_secs_f64() * 1000.0),\n+        format!(\"Memory: {:.1}MB\", debug.memory_usage.map(|m| m.resident as f64 / 1_000_000.0).unwrap_or(0.0)),\n+        format!(\"Glyphs cached: {}\", debug.glyphs_cached),\n+    ];\n+    \n+    // Render semi-transparent overlay in corner\n+    render_debug_panel(&lines, ctx);\n+}\n+```\n+\n+### Integration with Existing Features\n+\n+| Feature | Perf Metric | Why It Matters |\n+|---------|-------------|----------------|\n+| Soft wrapping | Visual index build time | O(n) on viewport resize |\n+| Syntax highlighting | Token cache hit rate | Avoid re-tokenizing |\n+| Large files | Memory growth over time | Detect leaks |\n+| Undo/redo | Stack memory usage | Bound history size |\n+| Scrolling | Frame drops during scroll | Smooth 60fps target |\n+\n+### Compile-Time Gating\n+\n+Keep perf monitoring optional to avoid overhead in release builds:\n+\n+```toml\n+# Cargo.toml\n+[features]\n+default = []\n+perf = [\"perf-monitor\"]\n+\n+[dependencies]\n+perf-monitor = { version = \"0.2\", optional = true }\n+```\n+\n+```rust\n+#[cfg(feature = \"perf\")]\n+mod debug;\n+\n+#[cfg(feature = \"perf\")]\n+use debug::DebugState;\n+\n+#[cfg(not(feature = \"perf\"))]\n+struct DebugState; // Zero-sized stub\n+```\n+\n+### Summary\n+\n+Performance monitoring fits naturally into the Elm architecture:\n+\n+| Aspect | Integration Point |\n+|--------|-------------------|\n+| **State** | `DebugState` in `AppModel` |\n+| **Collection** | `Cmd::StartPerfSampler` spawns background thread |\n+| **Updates** | `DebugMsg::UpdatePerfStats` posts samples to main loop |\n+| **Instrumentation** | Wrap `update()` with timing |\n+| **Display** | Overlay or status bar via `UiState` |\n+| **Toggle** | `DebugMsg::TogglePerfOverlay` bound to F12 |\n+\n+The unidirectional flow makes this clean: metrics collection is a `Cmd`, metric updates are `Msg`, and display is just another part of `view`.\n+\n+---\n+\n+## 6. What's Missing vs Production Editors\n+\n | Feature | Status | Priority | Effort |\n |---------|--------|----------|--------|\n | Multi-cursor | Missing | High | M |\n | Selections | Missing | High | M |\n````",

```
1) TL;DR
Use a single YAML theme file parsed with `serde_yaml` into a `Theme` struct.
Define a small, semantic schema: `meta`, `ui` (components + stateful colors), and `syntax` (token kinds + styles).
At render time, resolve the current state (normal/hover/active/focused/disabled) and read all colors from the loaded `Theme` instead of hardcoding them.

---

2) Recommended file format (simple path)

**Use YAML** for the theme files.

**Rationale:**

- Human-friendly for designers/devs: nesting and comments make complex UIs manageable.
- Maps cleanly to Rust structs via `serde_yaml`.
- Easier to extend than `key=value` and easier to hand-edit than JSON (no trailing-comma issues, supports comments, etc.).
- Keeps the format simple enough for a “minimal” editor but flexible enough for future features.

If you ever need machine-generated themes or editor integration, JSON can be trivially supported in parallel with the same Rust structs.

---

3) Naming conventions (clear & extensible)

**General rules:**

- **snake_case** for all keys: aligns w/ Rust field names.
- **Semantic over visual** names (e.g., `editor.background` instead of `black_background`).
- Components are **nouns**; states are **adjectives** nested under them.
- Syntax tokens are **language-agnostic roles**, not language-specific names
  (e.g., `keyword`, `type`, `function`, `string`, `comment`, `number`).

**Component naming pattern:**

- `ui.window.*`
- `ui.editor.*`
- `ui.gutter.*`
- `ui.scrollbar.*`
- `ui.minimap.*` (if you add one later)
- `ui.status_bar.*`
- `ui.panel.*` (sidebars, popups)
- `ui.popup.*`
- `ui.search.*`
- `ui.diagnostics.*`

**State naming pattern:**

For any element that has stateful visuals, use:

```yaml
<component>:
  <property>:
    normal:  "#RRGGBB"
    hover:   "#RRGGBB"   # optional
    active:  "#RRGGBB"   # optional
    focused: "#RRGGBB"   # optional
    disabled:"#RRGGBB"   # optional
```

e.g., `ui.editor.background`, `ui.scrollbar.thumb`, `ui.cursor.color`.

If a state key is missing, you fall back to `normal`.

**Syntax token naming pattern:**

```yaml
syntax:
  comment:
  keyword:
  keyword_control:
  keyword_declaration:
  type:
  type_builtin:
  function:
  function_builtin:
  variable:
  parameter:
  property:
  number:
  string:
  string_interpolation:
  boolean:
  operator:
  punctuation:
  macro:
  attribute:
  namespace:
  diagnostic_error:
  diagnostic_warning:
  diagnostic_hint:
```

Each syntax entry has:

```yaml
<token>:
  foreground: "#RRGGBB"
  background: "#RRGGBB" # optional
  font_style: ["italic", "bold"] # optional list: normal|bold|italic|underline|strikethrough
```

---

4) Theme schema (keys you need)

Below is the **schema structure**, not an actual theme. It shows all the keys and nesting.
You’ll map this directly to Rust structs.

```yaml
version: 1
name: "Fleet Dark"
author: "Example"
description: "A Fleet/IntelliJ-like dark theme"

ui:
  window:
    background: "#0F1419"
    border: "#181F26"

  editor:
    background:
      normal: "#0F1419"
      focused: "#101820"
      disabled: "#141922"
    foreground: "#D8DEE9"
    caret_row_background: "#151C24"      # current line highlight
    invisible_symbols: "#3C4454"         # e.g. whitespace guides
    selection:
      background:
        normal: "#214283"
        inactive: "#1A2E4A"
      border: "#2F5AAF"
    cursor:
      color:
        normal: "#FFCC00"
        overwrite: "#FF9933"
        read_only: "#7F7F7F"
    indent_guides:
      normal: "#242D38"
      active: "#3A4655"

  gutter:
    background:
      normal: "#0B1015"
      focused: "#0D1218"
    foreground: "#4B5665"
    foreground_active: "#8E9AB3"        # current line number
    separator: "#181F26"
    line_number_error: "#FF6188"
    line_number_warning: "#FFD866"

  scrollbar:
    track:
      background: "#0F1419"
    thumb:
      background:
        normal: "#343C4A"
        hover: "#3E4656"
        active: "#4A5567"

  minimap:
    background: "#0B1015"
    viewport: "#29323F"
    code: "#465066"

  status_bar:
    background: "#181F26"
    foreground: "#B0BECF"
    border: "#222B35"

  panel:
    background: "#131922"
    foreground: "#D8DEE9"
    border: "#181F26"
    title: "#8E9AB3"

  popup:
    background: "#1A212B"
    foreground: "#D8DEE9"
    border: "#2A3442"
    shadow: "#000000A0"

  search:
    match:
      background: "#3A4A63"
      border: "#5072C8"
    match_selected:
      background: "#425884"
      border: "#6C8DFF"

  diagnostics:
    error:
      foreground: "#FF6188"
      underline: "#FF6188"
      gutter_icon: "#FF6188"
    warning:
      foreground: "#FFD866"
      underline: "#FFD866"
      gutter_icon: "#FFD866"
    info:
      foreground: "#78DCE8"
      underline: "#78DCE8"
      gutter_icon: "#78DCE8"
    hint:
      foreground: "#A9B6D3"
      underline: "#A9B6D3"
      gutter_icon: "#A9B6D3"

syntax:
  comment:
    foreground: "#5C6370"
    font_style: ["italic"]

  keyword:
    foreground: "#C792EA"
    font_style: ["bold"]

  keyword_control:
    foreground: "#C792EA"
    font_style: ["bold"]

  keyword_declaration:
    foreground: "#FFCB6B"
    font_style: ["bold"]

  type:
    foreground: "#82AAFF"

  type_builtin:
    foreground: "#B2CCD6"

  function:
    foreground: "#82AAFF"

  function_builtin:
    foreground: "#61AFEF"

  variable:
    foreground: "#D8DEE9"

  parameter:
    foreground: "#E06C75"
    font_style: ["italic"]

  property:
    foreground: "#C0CAF5"

  number:
    foreground: "#F78C6C"

  string:
    foreground: "#C3E88D"

  string_interpolation:
    foreground: "#C3E88D"
    font_style: ["italic"]

  boolean:
    foreground: "#FF9CAC"

  operator:
    foreground: "#89DDFF"

  punctuation:
    foreground: "#89A7B1"

  macro:
    foreground: "#FFCB6B"
    font_style: ["bold"]

  attribute:
    foreground: "#FF9CAC"
    font_style: ["italic"]

  namespace:
    foreground: "#B2CCD6"

  diagnostic_error:
    foreground: "#FF6188"
    font_style: ["underline"]

  diagnostic_warning:
    foreground: "#FFD866"
    font_style: ["underline"]

  diagnostic_hint:
    foreground: "#A9B6D3"
    font_style: ["italic"]

  invalid:
    foreground: "#FFFFFF"
    background: "#FF000055"
```

You don’t have to implement every single key on day one; it’s okay if some are unused until you wire them up.

6) How this integrates with the Rust editor

Effort: **M (1–3h)** if you’re already using `serde`; **L** if you need to propagate theme everywhere.

### 6.1 Data structures (Rust)

Use `serde` and `serde_yaml`:

```toml
# Cargo.toml
[dependencies]
serde = { version = "1", features = ["derive"] }
serde_yaml = "0.9"
```

Define core types (simplified; you can add fields as needed):

```rust
use serde::Deserialize;

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy)]
pub struct Color(pub u8, pub u8, pub u8, pub u8); // RGBA

fn parse_hex_color(s: &str) -> Result<Color, String> {
    let s = s.trim_start_matches('#');
    let (r, g, b, a) = match s.len() {
        6 => {
            let r = u8::from_str_radix(&s[0..2], 16).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
            let g = u8::from_str_radix(&s[2..4], 16).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
            let b = u8::from_str_radix(&s[4..6], 16).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
            (r, g, b, 0xFF)
        }
        8 => {
            let r = u8::from_str_radix(&s[0..2], 16).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
            let g = u8::from_str_radix(&s[2..4], 16).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
            let b = u8::from_str_radix(&s[4..6], 16).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
            let a = u8::from_str_radix(&s[6..8], 16).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
            (r, g, b, a)
        }
        _ => return Err(format!("Invalid color: {}", s)),
    };
    Ok(Color(r, g, b, a))
}

mod de_color {
    use super::*;
    use serde::{Deserialize, Deserializer};

    pub fn deserialize<'de, D>(deserializer: D) -> Result<Color, D::Error>
    where
        D: Deserializer<'de>,
    {
        let s = String::deserialize(deserializer)?;
        parse_hex_color(&s).map_err(serde::de::Error::custom)
    }
}

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Deserialize)]
pub struct StatefulColor {
    #[serde(with = "de_color")]
    pub normal: Color,
    #[serde(default, with = "de_color", skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
    pub hover: Option<Color>,
    #[serde(default, with = "de_color", skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
    pub active: Option<Color>,
    #[serde(default, with = "de_color", skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
    pub focused: Option<Color>,
    #[serde(default, with = "de_color", skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
    pub disabled: Option<Color>,
}

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Deserialize)]
pub struct EditorUiTheme {
    pub background: StatefulColor,
    #[serde(with = "de_color")]
    pub foreground: Color,
    #[serde(with = "de_color")]
    pub caret_row_background: Color,
    #[serde(with = "de_color")]
    pub invisible_symbols: Color,
    pub selection: EditorSelectionTheme,
    pub cursor: CursorTheme,
    pub indent_guides: IndentGuidesTheme,
}

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Deserialize)]
pub struct EditorSelectionTheme {
    pub background: SelectionBackgroundTheme,
    #[serde(with = "de_color")]
    pub border: Color,
}

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Deserialize)]
pub struct SelectionBackgroundTheme {
    #[serde(with = "de_color")]
    pub normal: Color,
    #[serde(with = "de_color")]
    pub inactive: Color,
}

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Deserialize)]
pub struct CursorTheme {
    pub color: CursorColorTheme,
}

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Deserialize)]
pub struct CursorColorTheme {
    #[serde(with = "de_color")]
    pub normal: Color,
    #[serde(with = "de_color")]
    pub overwrite: Color,
    #[serde(with = "de_color")]
    pub read_only: Color,
}

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Deserialize)]
pub struct IndentGuidesTheme {
    #[serde(with = "de_color")]
    pub normal: Color,
    #[serde(with = "de_color")]
    pub active: Color,
}

// … similar structs for GutterUiTheme, ScrollbarTheme, etc.

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Deserialize)]
pub struct SyntaxStyle {
    #[serde(with = "de_color")]
    pub foreground: Color,
    #[serde(default, with = "de_color", skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
    pub background: Option<Color>,
    #[serde(default)]
    pub font_style: Vec<String>, // or a custom enum/bitflags
}

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Deserialize)]
pub struct SyntaxTheme {
    pub comment: SyntaxStyle,
    pub keyword: SyntaxStyle,
    pub keyword_control: SyntaxStyle,
    pub keyword_declaration: SyntaxStyle,
    pub r#type: SyntaxStyle,
    pub type_builtin: SyntaxStyle,
    pub function: SyntaxStyle,
    pub function_builtin: SyntaxStyle,
    pub variable: SyntaxStyle,
    pub parameter: SyntaxStyle,
    pub property: SyntaxStyle,
    pub number: SyntaxStyle,
    pub string: SyntaxStyle,
    pub string_interpolation: SyntaxStyle,
    pub boolean: SyntaxStyle,
    pub operator: SyntaxStyle,
    pub punctuation: SyntaxStyle,
    pub macro_: SyntaxStyle,
    pub attribute: SyntaxStyle,
    pub namespace: SyntaxStyle,
    pub diagnostic_error: SyntaxStyle,
    pub diagnostic_warning: SyntaxStyle,
    pub diagnostic_hint: SyntaxStyle,
    pub invalid: SyntaxStyle,
}

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Deserialize)]
pub struct UiTheme {
    pub window: WindowTheme,
    pub editor: EditorUiTheme,
    // gutter, scrollbar, minimap, etc.
}

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Deserialize)]
pub struct WindowTheme {
    #[serde(with = "de_color")]
    pub background: Color,
    #[serde(with = "de_color")]
    pub border: Color,
}

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Deserialize)]
pub struct Theme {
    pub version: u32,
    pub name: String,
    pub author: String,
    pub description: Option<String>,
    pub ui: UiTheme,
    pub syntax: SyntaxTheme,
}
```

You don’t need to implement every struct up front—start with the ones you actually use (e.g., `window`, `editor`, `syntax.comment`, `syntax.keyword`, etc.) and expand gradually.

### 6.2 Loading the theme

```rust
use std::fs;

pub fn load_theme(path: &str) -> Result<Theme, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
    let text = fs::read_to_string(path)?;
    let theme: Theme = serde_yaml::from_str(&text)?;
    Ok(theme)
}
```

Call this once at startup or when the user switches themes, then keep a `Theme` instance in some `Arc<Theme>` accessible by the rendering code.

### 6.3 Applying the theme (UI & syntax)

**UI path (gutter, viewport, cursor, etc.):**

- Replace all hard-coded colors like `0xFF1E1E1E` with references into `theme.ui`.
  - viewport background: `theme.ui.editor.background.normal` (or `.focused` if active)
  - gutter background: `theme.ui.gutter.background.normal`
  - line numbers: `theme.ui.gutter.foreground` / `.foreground_active`
  - selection rect: `theme.ui.editor.selection.background.normal`
  - cursor: `theme.ui.editor.cursor.color.normal`

**State resolution helper:**

```rust
pub enum UiState {
    Normal,
    Hover,
    Active,
    Focused,
    Disabled,
}

impl StatefulColor {
    pub fn resolve(&self, state: UiState) -> Color {
        match state {
            UiState::Hover => self.hover.unwrap_or(self.normal),
            UiState::Active => self.active.unwrap_or(self.normal),
            UiState::Focused => self.focused.unwrap_or(self.normal),
            UiState::Disabled => self.disabled.unwrap_or(self.normal),
            UiState::Normal => self.normal,
        }
    }
}
```

Your event/input system (hover/press/focus) determines `UiState` for each component; the renderer calls `resolve` to get the actual color.

**Syntax path:**

- Map your high-level token categories to `syntax` entries:
  - parser/lexer/syntax tree yields something like `TokenKind::Keyword`, `TokenKind::Identifier`, etc.
  - the highlighter resolves them to a semantic category (e.g., `SyntaxRole::KeywordControl`, `SyntaxRole::TypeBuiltin`, `SyntaxRole::Function`).
  - For each `SyntaxRole`, pick the relevant `SyntaxStyle` from `theme.syntax`.

Example:

```rust
pub enum SyntaxRole {
    Comment,
    Keyword,
    KeywordControl,
    KeywordDeclaration,
    Type,
    TypeBuiltin,
    Function,
    FunctionBuiltin,
    Variable,
    Parameter,
    Property,
    Number,
    String,
    StringInterpolation,
    Boolean,
    Operator,
    Punctuation,
    Macro,
    Attribute,
    Namespace,
    DiagnosticError,
    DiagnosticWarning,
    DiagnosticHint,
    Invalid,
}

impl SyntaxTheme {
    pub fn style_for(&self, role: SyntaxRole) -> &SyntaxStyle {
        match role {
            SyntaxRole::Comment => &self.comment,
            SyntaxRole::Keyword => &self.keyword,
            SyntaxRole::KeywordControl => &self.keyword_control,
            SyntaxRole::KeywordDeclaration => &self.keyword_declaration,
            SyntaxRole::Type => &self.r#type,
            SyntaxRole::TypeBuiltin => &self.type_builtin,
            SyntaxRole::Function => &self.function,
            SyntaxRole::FunctionBuiltin => &self.function_builtin,
            SyntaxRole::Variable => &self.variable,
            SyntaxRole::Parameter => &self.parameter,
            SyntaxRole::Property => &self.property,
            SyntaxRole::Number => &self.number,
            SyntaxRole::String => &self.string,
            SyntaxRole::StringInterpolation => &self.string_interpolation,
            SyntaxRole::Boolean => &self.boolean,
            SyntaxRole::Operator => &self.operator,
            SyntaxRole::Punctuation => &self.punctuation,
            SyntaxRole::Macro => &self.macro_,
            SyntaxRole::Attribute => &self.attribute,
            SyntaxRole::Namespace => &self.namespace,
            SyntaxRole::DiagnosticError => &self.diagnostic_error,
            SyntaxRole::DiagnosticWarning => &self.diagnostic_warning,
            SyntaxRole::DiagnosticHint => &self.diagnostic_hint,
            SyntaxRole::Invalid => &self.invalid,
        }
    }
}
```

Your text rendering pass uses the `foreground`/`background` colors and `font_style` flags from `SyntaxStyle`.

---

7) Rationale & trade-offs

- YAML + `serde` is minimal effort but gives you room to grow (palettes, derived colors, etc.).
- Semantic names decouple the theme from implementation details; you can tweak styles without code changes.
- State modifiers are localized to components that need them instead of a global overridable mess.

Alternatives (not needed now):

- Flat `key=value` files are too limited for nested UI + modifiers.
- A full-blown palette system with references (e.g., `color: "accent_primary"`) is nice but adds a lookup layer—add it only if you see duplication.

---

8) Risks & guardrails

- **Missing fields**: define sensible defaults in Rust or ship a built-in theme to fall back to if parsing fails.
- **Invalid colors**: validate on load and report a clear error (file + key).
- **Drift from UI glossary**: keep your schema aligned with `EDITOR_UI_REFERENCE.md`; if you rename components there, reflect it in `ui.*` keys.

---

9) When to consider the advanced path

Upgrade to a more complex design when:

- You support **user-created themes** and want **color references/palettes** to avoid repetition.
- You add **light themes** and need contrast checks or automatic derived colors (borders, hovers).
- You localize your editor UI and want theme packs to be shareable across platforms.

Optional advanced path (outline only):

- Introduce a `palette` section (e.g., `palette.accent_primary`) and allow UI/syntax entries to refer to named colors.
- Add a small expression language or “variants” (`accent_primary/hover`) resolved at load time.
- Support both YAML and JSON with the same `Theme` struct for ecosystem compatibility.

For now, the YAML + semantic keys + simple stateful colors is the clean, minimal, and extensible solution.
```

Press `⌘ ⇧ K` , start typing `Edit Color` , and select Edit Color Theme from the list.\n2. A separate tab opens in the editor. Tweak the values in the theme file as required and give it a name. [textAttributes](modifying-theme-settings.html) (optional): text attributes used for syntax highlighting in the editor.\n    4. It is applied when there is a need to visually separate some information from the primary background.\n[](https://resources.jetbrains.com/help/img/fleet/1.48/fleet_background_section_json_theme_plugin.png)\n\n|Key |Value |Type |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n|`background.primary` |`Neutral_150` |`string` |\n|`background.secondary` |`Gray 110` |`string` |\n\n### banner\n\nBanners are visual components used to display important messages or statuses. #### Types of banners\n\nThere are two types of banners:\n\n* Floating banner ( `banner. *` ): shown in the top-middle part of the screen. [](https://resources.jetbrains.com/help/img/fleet/1.48/fleet_button_secondary_section_json_theme_plugin.png)\n* `warning` : highlight cautionary or important actions. [](https://resources.jetbrains.com/help/img/fleet/1.48/fleet_button_warning_section_json_theme_plugin.png)\n* `ai` : used for AI-specific operations. [](https://resources.jetbrains.com/help/img/fleet/1.48/fleet_button_ai_section_json_theme_plugin.png)\n\n#### Styling components\n\nEach button type has specific keys for different styling components and states:\n\n* `background` : defines the background color for default, hovered, pressed, and disabled states. * `border` : configures the border color for various states, including focused borders. * `focus` : highlights the button outline when it is in focus.\n ... \n|\n|`combobox.text.disabled` |`Neutral_110` |`string` |\n|`combobox.text.error` |`Neutral_10` |`string` |\n|`combobox.text.hovered` |`Neutral_10` |`string` |\n\n### completion\n\nThe completion list is a UI component that displays a list of suggestions for the current context. #### Styling Elements\n\nThe completion list includes the following visual elements:\n\n* `text` : the color of the text. * `background` : the color of the text highlight. For example, you can drag a tab to position it below another tab or beside it.\n ... \n|`string` |\n\n### editor\n\nThe `editor. *` keys configure colors for various editor elements, including line numbers, the caret, code foldings, and Git information about changed lines in the gutter. The following screenshot demonstrates different color modifications for the `editor. *` keys. The modified keys are listed below:\n\n* `\"editor.caret.background\": \"Pink\"` : changes the caret's background color to `Pink` . * `\"editor.currentLine.background.default\": \"Pink\"` : colors the background of the currently selected line in `Pink` . * `\"editor.foldedMark.background\": \"\"` and `\"editor.foldedMark.text\": \"Green 70\"` : adjusts the colors for code folding marks. [](https://resources.jetbrains.com/help/img/fleet/1.48/fleet_spinner_section_json_theme_plugin.png)\n\n|Key |Value |Type |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n|`progressSpinner.foreground` |`Neutral_10` |`string` |\n\n### remote\n\nThe `remote.userColor. *` keys define user colors during a [collaborative development session](collaborative-development.html) . The user's ID hash code is used as a seed for randomization. * Predefined color set: uses a fixed set of 10 theme-defined colors, identified by the keys: `remote.userColor.background.one` through `remote.userColor.background.ten`\n  \n  Colors are selected from these theme keys. * Usage context: used primarily in user icons or avatars.\nThe colors serve as a background for circular user avatars and are paired with white text showing user initials.     "url": "https://www.jetbrains.com/help/fleet/modifying-theme-settings.html",
    "title": "Structure of the JSON format for themes | JetBrains Fleet",
    "excerpts": [
      "### JetBrains Fleet 1.48 Help\n\n# Structure of the JSON format for themes\n\nThis topic explains the structure of the JSON theme file. This JSON file contains mappings between different UI elements and their assigned colors, as well as other theme settings.\n\nThemes are defined in JSON files and contain four blocks:\n\n1. [meta]() : metadata, such as the theme's name and type.\n2. [colors]() : colors for UI elements, such as buttons, headers, and backgrounds.\n3. [textAttributes]() (optional): text attributes used for syntax highlighting in the editor.\n4. [palette]() (optional): named colors that can be used in the `colors` and `textAttributes` blocks.\n\n## Supported color formats\n\nThe following color formats are supported, each offering specific ways to define colors with varying degrees of opacity:\n\n* `` : this format represents colors using three pairs of hexadecimal digits, where each pair corresponds to the red, green, and blue components of the color. Each component can have a value ranging from 00 to FF, which corresponds to the intensity of that color component.\n  \n  Example: ``\n  \n    + Red: `7f` (127 in decimal, indicating a medium intensity of red).\n    + Green: `7f` (127 in decimal, indicating a medium intensity of green).\n    + Blue: `7f` (127 in decimal, indicating a medium intensity of blue).\n  \n  This format does not include an alpha channel, meaning it is fully opaque.\n* `` : this format is an extension of the `` format, with an additional pair of hexadecimal digits to represent the alpha channel, which defines the opacity of the color.\n  \n  Example: `` .\n  \n    + Red: `7f` (127 in decimal).\n    + Green: `7f` (127 in decimal).\n    + Blue: `7f` (127 in decimal).\n    + Alpha: `ff` (255 in decimal, indicating full opacity).\n* `` : similar to `` , this format uses uppercase letters to denote the hexadecimal digits, which is purely stylistic and has no effect on the color itself.\n  \n  Example: `` .\n* `` : similar to `` , this format uses uppercase letters to denote the hexadecimal digits, which is purely stylistic and has no effect on the color itself.\n  \n  Example: `` .\n\n## meta\n\nDefines mandatory theme properties.\n\nExample:\n\n{\n\"meta\": {\n\"theme.name\": \"CustomName\", // Theme name visible to the user in the theme selection menu [String]\n\"theme.kind\": \"Light\" // Determines the fallback theme for missing colors. Options: [Light, Dark]\n}\n}\n\n## colors\n\nExample:\n\n{\n\"colors\": {\n\"dragAndDrop.background\": \"\",\n\"editor.background\": \"White\" // Named color from the 'palette' section is used instead of a hex code\n}\n}\n\n## textAttributes\n\nDefines text attributes used for syntax highlighting in the editor. This block is optional.\n\nThe following fallback rules are applied to this block:\n\n* If the `textAttributes` block is missing, the text attributes from the default Light or Dark theme are used (depending on the `theme.kind` key in `meta` ).\n* If the `textAttributes` block is present, text attributes will not fall back to the parent theme. If an attribute is not found, it tries to fall back on another attribute in the same theme by dropping the last segment. If there is no fallback, the default text color is used.\n\nFallback example: `\"problem.warning.weak\"` \\-> `\"problem.warning\"` \\-> `\"problem\"` \\-> using default text color\n\nExample:\n\n{\n\"attributeName\": {\n\"foregroundColor\": \"Gray 10\", // Text color [String in #RRGGBBAA format or named color from the palette]\n\"backgroundColor\": \"Gray 10\", // Background color [String in #RRGGBBAA format or named color from the palette]\n\"statusColor\": \"Gray 10\", // Scrollbar mark color to the right of the text [String in #RRGGBBAA format or named color from the palette]\n\"fontWeight\": \"BOLD\", // Font weight, options: [NORMAL, BOLD, SEMI\\_BOLD]\n\"fontStyle\": \"ITALIC\", // Font style, options: [NORMAL, ITALIC]\n\"textDecoration\": {\n\"color\": \"Gray 10\", // Decoration color, overrides foregroundColor\n\"style\": \"SOLID\", // Decoration style, options: [SOLID, DOUBLE, DOTTED, DASHED, WAVY]\n\"type\": \"UNDERLINE\", // Decoration position relative to text, options: [OVERLINE, LINE\\_THROUGH, UNDERLINE]\n\"thickness\": 1.0 // Decoration thickness [Double]\n},\n\"border\": {\n\"color\": \"Gray 10\", // Border color [String in #RRGGBBAA format or named color from the palette], overrides foregroundColor\n\"thickness\": 1.0 // Border thickness [Double]\n}\n}\n}\n\nExample:\n\n{\n\"textAttributes\": {\n\"editor.selection\": {\n\"backgroundColor\": \"Gray 80\"\n},\n\"editor.selection.focused\": {\n\"backgroundColor\": \"\"\n},\n\"editor.indentGuide\": {\n\"foregroundColor\": \"Gray 90\",\n\"textDecoration\": {\n\"style\": \"DASHED\"\n}\n},\n\"problem.error\": {\n\"textDecoration\": {\n\"color\": \"\",\n\"style\": \"SOLID\",\n\"type\": \"UNDERLINE\",\n\"thickness\": 2.0\n}\n}\n}\n}\n\n## palette\n\nDefines named colors that can be used in the theme. This block is optional.\n\nExample:\n\n\"colors\": {\n\"button.positive.text.default\": \"TextDefault\",\n\"button.primary.background.default\": \"BackgroundDefault\"\n},\n\"palette\": {\n\"TextDefault\": \"\",\n\"BackgroundDefault\": \"\"\n}\n\n## Example\n\nHere is an example of a simple theme that changes colors in the code editor:\n\n{\n\"meta\": {\n\"theme.name\": \"Quantum Harmony\",\n\"theme.kind\": \"Light\"\n},\n\"textAttributes\": {\n\"editor.selection\": {\n\"backgroundColor\": \"\"\n},\n\"editor.selection.focused\": {\n\"backgroundColor\": \"\"\n},\n\"editor.indentGuide\": {\n\"foregroundColor\": \"\",\n\"textDecoration\": {\n\"style\": \"DASHED\"\n}\n},\n\"problem.error\": {\n\"textDecoration\": {\n\"color\": \"\",\n\"style\": \"SOLID\",\n\"type\": \"UNDERLINE\",\n\"thickness\": 2.0\n}\n}\n},\n\"colors\": {\n\"tab.background.default\": \"\",\n\"tab.background.hovered\": \"\",\n\"tab.background.selected\": \"\",\n\"tab.indicator\": \"IndicatorColor\",\n\"popup.tab.background.default\": \"\",\n\"popup.tab.background.hovered\": \"\",\n\"popup.tab.background.selected\": \"\",\n\"popup.tab.indicator\": \"IndicatorColor\",\n\"editor.background\": \"\",\n\"editor.caret.background\": \"\",\n\"editor.caret.border\": \"\",\n\"tool.background\": \"\"\n},\n\"palette\": {\n\"IndicatorColor\": \"\"\n}\n}\n\n06 May 2025\n\n[Quick start guide: building a theme plugin in Fleet](building-custom-theme-plugins.html) [Key changes to theme plugin keys](changes-in-json-keys-for-theme-plugins.html)\n"
    ]

One Dark Pro is a professional and elegant theme with a dark background and contrasting colors. This color scheme is designed to make your code easy to read and visually appealing.\n\n## Instalation\n\n[]()\n\nTo install the One Dark Pro color scheme in JetBrains Fleet, follow these steps:\n\n1.   "content": "# Theming System Design\n\nA pragmatic, YAML-based theming system inspired by JetBrains Fleet's JSON theme format.\n\n---\n\n## Overview\n\n**Goals:**\n- Simple file format (YAML) that's human-editable\n- Comprehensive coverage of all UI components\n- State modifiers (hover, active, focused, disabled)\n- Semantic naming aligned with `EDITOR_UI_REFERENCE.md` glossary\n- Easy to parse in Rust with `serde`\n\n**Non-goals (for now):**\n- Color palette references/aliases (add later if needed)\n- Light theme support (dark-only initially)\n- Theme inheritance/extending\n\n---\n\n## File Format: YAML\n\n**Why YAML over JSON or key=value?**\n\n| Format | Pros | Cons |\n|--------|------|------|\n| **YAML** | Comments, readable nesting, no trailing commas | Whitespace-sensitive |\n| JSON | Fleet uses it, universal | No comments, verbose |\n| key=value | Simple | No nesting, flat structure |\n\nYAML maps cleanly to Rust structs via `serde_yaml`, supports comments for documentation, and is easy to hand-edit.\n\n---\n\n## Complete Theme Schema\n\n### Structure\n\n```yaml\nversion: 1                    # Schema version for future migrations\nname: \"Theme Name\"\nauthor: \"Author Name\"\ndescription: \"Optional description\"\n\nui:                           # All UI component colors\n  window: { ... }\n  editor: { ... }\n  gutter: { ... }\n  scrollbar: { ... }\n  status_bar: { ... }\n  popup: { ... }\n  search: { ... }\n  diagnostics: { ... }\n\nsyntax:                       # Syntax highlighting colors\n  comment: { ... }\n  keyword: { ... }\n  # ... etc\n```\n\n### Naming Conventions\n\n1. **snake_case** for all keys (matches Rust field names)\n2. **Semantic over visual** names (`editor.background` not `dark_gray`)\n3. **Components are nouns**, states are nested under them\n4. **Hierarchy**: `component.subcomponent.property.state`\n\n### State Modifiers\n\nFor interactive elements, states are nested:\n\n```yaml\nbackground:\n  normal: \"#1E1E1E\"      # Default state\n  hover: \"#2A2A2A\"       # Mouse hovering\n  active: \"#3A3A3A\"      # Being pressed/activated\n  focused: \"#252525\"     # Has keyboard focus\n  disabled: \"#151515\"    # Grayed out\n```\n\nIf a state is missing, fall back to `normal`.\n\n---\n\n## Complete Key Reference\n\n### UI Components\n\nBased on `EDITOR_UI_REFERENCE.md` glossary with state modifiers where Dependencies\n\n```toml\n[dependencies]\nserde = { version = \"1\", features = [\"derive\"] }\nserde_yaml = \"0.9\"\n```\n\n### Core Types\n\n```rust\nuse serde::Deserialize;\n\n/// RGBA color (0-255 per channel)\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, Default)]\npub struct Color {\n    pub r: u8,\n    pub g: u8,\n    pub b: u8,\n    pub a: u8,\n}\n\nimpl Color {\n    pub fn to_argb_u32(&self) -> u32 {\n        ((self.a as u32) << 24) | ((self.r as u32) << 16) | ((self.g as u32) << 8) | (self.b as u32)\n    }\n}\n\n/// Parse \"#RRGGBB\" or \"#RRGGBBAA\"\nfn parse_color(s: &str) -> Result<Color, String> {\n    let s = s.trim_start_matches('#');\n    match s.len() {\n        6 => Ok(Color {\n            r: u8::from_str_radix(&s[0..2], 16).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?,\n            g: u8::from_str_radix(&s[2..4], 16).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?,\n            b: u8::from_str_radix(&s[4..6], 16).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?,\n            a: 255,\n        }),\n        8 => Ok(Color {\n            r: u8::from_str_radix(&s[0..2], 16).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?,\n            g: u8::from_str_radix(&s[2..4], 16).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?,\n            b: u8::from_str_radix(&s[4..6], 16).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?,\n            a: u8::from_str_radix(&s[6..8], 16).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?,\n        }),\n        _ => Err(format!(\"Invalid color format: {}\", s)),\n    }\n}\n\n/// Stateful color (for components with hover/active/etc. states)\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Deserialize)]\npub struct StatefulColor {\n    pub normal: String,\n    pub hover: Option<String>,\n    pub active: Option<String>,\n    pub focused: Option<String>,\n    pub disabled: Option<String>,\n}\n\nimpl StatefulColor {\n    pub fn resolve(&self, state: UiState) -> Color {\n        let hex = match state {\n            UiState::Hover => self.hover.as_ref().unwrap_or(&self.normal),\n            UiState::Active => self.active.as_ref().unwrap_or(&self.normal),\n            UiState::Focused => self.focused.as_ref().unwrap_or(&self.normal),\n            UiState::Disabled => self.disabled.as_ref().unwrap_or(&self.normal),\n            UiState::Normal => &self.normal,\n        };\n        parse_color(hex).unwrap_or_default()\n    }\n}\n\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy)]\npub enum UiState {\n    Normal,\n    Hover,\n    Active,\n    Focused,\n    Disabled,\n}\n\n/// Syntax style entry\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Deserialize)]\npub struct SyntaxStyle {\n    pub foreground: String,\n    pub background: Option<String>,\n    #[serde(default)]\n    pub font_style: Vec<String>,\n}\n\nimpl SyntaxStyle {\n    pub fn foreground_color(&self) -> Color {\n        parse_color(&self.foreground).unwrap_or_default()\n    }\n    \n    pub fn is_bold(&self) -> bool {\n        self.font_style.iter().any(|s| s == \"bold\")\n    }\n    \n    pub fn is_italic(&self) -> bool {\n        self.font_style.iter().any(|s| s == \"italic\")\n    }\n}\n```\n\n### Loading Themes\n\n```rust\nuse std::fs;\nuse std::path::Path;\n\npub fn load_theme(path: &Path) -> Result<Theme, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    let content = fs::read_to_string(path)?;\n    let theme: Theme = serde_yaml::from_str(&content)?;\n    Ok(theme)\n}\n\n// In your Model/AppModel\npub struct AppModel {\n    // ... other fields\n    pub theme: Theme,\n}\n\n// At startup\nlet theme = load_theme(Path::new(\"themes/fleet-dark.yaml\"))\n    .unwrap_or_else(|_| Theme::default_dark());\n```\n\n### Using in Renderer\n\n```rust\n// Before (hardcoded)\nconst BACKGROUND: u32 = 0xFF1E1E1E;\nctx.fill_rect(0, 0, width, height, BACKGROUND);\n\n// After (themed)\nlet bg = theme.ui.editor.background.resolve(UiState::Normal);\nctx.fill_rect(0, 0, width, height, bg.to_argb_u32());\n\n// Syntax highlighting\nlet token_style = theme.syntax.style_for(SyntaxRole::Keyword);\nctx.set_color(token_style.foreground_color().to_argb_u32());\nif token_style.is_bold() {\n    ctx.set_font_weight(FontWeight::Bold);\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n## Architecture Integration\n\nTheming fits into the recommended architecture as part of `AppModel`:\n\n```rust\nstruct AppModel {\n    documents: HashMap<DocumentId, Document>,\n    editors: HashMap<EditorId, EditorState>,\n    ui: UiState,\n    debug: DebugState,\n    theme: Theme,  // ← Theme lives here\n}\n\nenum Msg {\n    // ...\n    Theme(ThemeMsg),\n}\n\nenum ThemeMsg {\n    Load(PathBuf),\n    LoadCompleted(Result<Theme, String>),\n    Reload,\n}\n\nenum Cmd {\n    // ...\n    LoadTheme { path: PathBuf },\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n## File Locations\n\n```\n~/.config/your-editor/\n├── config.yaml           # General settings\n└── themes/\n    ├── fleet-dark.yaml   # Built-in\n    ├── github-dark.yaml  # Built-in  \n    └── my-theme.yaml     # User-created\n```\n\n---\n\n## Future Enhancements (Not Required Now)\n\n1. **Color palette/aliases**: Define `palette.accent` and reference it\n2. **Theme inheritance**: `extends: \"fleet-dark\"` for variants\n3. **Light theme support**: Automatic contrast adjustments\n4. **Hot reloading**: Watch theme file for changes\n5. **VS Code theme import**: Parse `.json` themes from VS Code\n\n---\n\n## Summary\n\n| Aspect | Decision |\n|--------|----------|\n| **Format** | YAML |\n| **Naming** | snake_case, semantic, hierarchical |\n| **States** | Nested under component: `normal`, `hover`, `active`, `focused`, `disabled` |\n| **Syntax** | Role-based: `keyword`, `function`, `type`, etc. |\n| **Parsing** | `serde_yaml` → Rust structs |\n| **Storage** | `theme: Theme` in `AppModel` |\n| **Messages** | `ThemeMsg::Load`, `ThemeMsg::LoadCompleted` |\n\nThis gives you a pragmatic, extensible theming system that covers all your UI needs while remaining simple to edit and maintain.\n"
}
